2026-08-23 · apps/web (wt-perf) — **The product was never waiting on JavaScript, and the number that proved it was the SHAPE of the data rather than any single figure.** Nobody had measured this app. Forty routes, three data states, two network conditions, against `next start` — and first contentful paint landed a flat ~40ms after time-to-first-byte on ALL FORTY, while total blocking time was **0ms at p50 and 100ms at worst under 4x CPU throttling**. A flat 40ms gap is not a property of forty different pages; it is one shared blocker, and it said the 187 kB bundle was innocent before any bundle was opened. The blocker was `currentUser()` in `RailFoot`: **p50 354ms, mean 417ms, 61% of ALL outbound wall time across the sweep**, spent on every page to render a display name. `auth()` reads the session JWT locally; `currentUser()` is a network round trip to api.clerk.com, and the two are one character apart at the call site. Because the rail is in the layout it sat on the critical path of every authenticated page — and this is why the existing `loading.tsx` skeletons could not help: `loading.tsx` gives the PAGE a boundary so the LAYOUT can flush around it, and the layout was itself blocked, so the shell that would paint the skeleton was stuck behind the very call the skeleton existed to cover. Streaming `RailFoot` behind Suspense took TTFB p50 **509 -> 170ms** and LCP p50 **552 -> 216ms** with the data source unchanged. Reading `users_profile.display_name` instead would have been faster still and WRONG: that row is written once by `bootstrap_workspace` and never refreshed, so a rename would show the old name forever — the fast answer and the correct one were different answers. **And the database was never the problem.** The app's own queries, planned through RLS as `authenticated` with a real member's claims, execute in **0.30-1.17ms while their measured wire time is 76-131ms** — DB execution is under 1.5% of the cost, which is why ZERO indexes were added: there was no query to speed up, only round trips to remove. (The RLS design is sound, and the plan proves it: `workspace_id IN (SELECT app.member_workspace_ids())` evaluates as a **hashed SubPlan run once**, not per row.) Three method notes worth keeping. **Instrument at `globalThis.fetch` via `node --require`, never at `createServerSupabase()`** — middleware calls PostgREST with a bare fetch, so a counter wired into one client certifies any route that used another, and it needs no product-code change to take the numbers. **A detector inherits the blind spot of the code it audits, twice over here**: a string-matching index sweep flagged four tables for missing a `workspace_id` index when they have no `workspace_id` COLUMN, and a page-only waterfall scanner was blind to the rail — which is where the entire defect lived. **A guard nobody has watched fail is not a guard**: two of the four break-proofs for `js-budget.mjs` reported GREEN on the first attempt because I had guessed the route-key format wrong, and the guard was cheerfully certifying a regression I had deliberately planted. The honest cost of the fix is recorded too — total blocking time across forty throttled routes went **677 -> 1772ms**, and a large part of that is definitional (TBT counts long tasks after FCP, and FCP moved 400ms earlier) but I did not record unfiltered long-task totals, so the split is stated as unmeasured rather than estimated. Two job defects found and deliberately NOT fixed: `listTargets()` takes the oldest 120 published variants with no window and no measured-today exclusion and nothing downstream filters them, so past 120 targets the same oldest 120 are re-selected every night forever and everything ranked 121st is never measured while the heartbeat reports green — the append-only table's reassuring growth number and the starvation bug are the SAME FACT, since the cap is what keeps the table small; and `/api/cron/loop` sets no `maxDuration` at all while doing the most work of any cron (40 workspaces sequentially, a model call each, a pg Pool created per workspace and `.end()` called nowhere in the file), where the two crons bounded by batch size both set 300.

2026-08-21 · apps/web + packages/publishing + packages/db (wt-webhooks) — **The whole webhook contract was in a place a `paths` count cannot see, and the count is the fingerprint of the mistake.** The brief said Zernio's document has "397 paths"; it has 399 — and, more importantly, a SEPARATE top-level `webhooks:` object holding **49 event definitions**, which OpenAPI 3.1 introduced precisely because 3.0 had nowhere to describe inbound events. Anyone who greps or counts `.paths` sees NONE of the contract, concludes it is undocumented, and starts inventing; that empty result is the vacuum a previous summarising model filled with `/v1/post` and `platformSpecificOptions`. Three findings from actually parsing it change how a receiver must be built. **The HMAC covers the RAW BODY AND NOTHING ELSE** — lowercase hex, no `sha256=` prefix, no timestamp in the signed material — so the Cashfree receiver's replay-window defence cannot be carried over, and carrying it over would actively break things: `payload.timestamp` is documented as pinned at event creation and "retries and redeliveries keep the original value", while retries run to ~51 hours, so a 5-minute freshness window would reject attempts 2 through 7 of exactly the events we failed to receive the first time. Replay is defended by a unique index, never by the clock. **`profileId` is on 18 of the 26 payload schemas and absent from every one this lane needed** — comment.received, review.new, review.updated, post._, post.platform._, lead.received. So doc 13 §11 Q6's "does the event carry the profile ID for workspace routing?" answers _yes, and uselessly_: a router built on that true summary silently drops every inbox and publish event. Routing is `accountId`, which is on 25 of 26 (all but `webhook.test`, which carries neither and is therefore un-routable by construction — which is why `workspace_id` on the events table is NULLABLE, against the standing rule, since NOT NULL would force the receiver to REJECT a delivery that can never succeed on retry, and a non-2xx means seven retries and a dead letter). **TWO OF THE BRIEF'S THREE PROMISED BENEFITS DO NOT SURVIVE CONTACT WITH THE CODE.** `realtime_publish_state` is not "the foundation of this" — it is a Supabase Realtime publication toggle, the delivery leg to the BROWSER, orthogonal to receiving anything from Zernio; applied anyway, on its own decision, because it removes a reload. And webhooks cannot fix the 73–199 s scheduled-post lag: `adapters/zernio.ts:325` sends `publishNow: true` on every call, so **Sahoda holds the schedule and Zernio never does** — Zernio does not know a scheduled post exists until our own cron decides to publish it, and docs/32 §P6 already decomposes that lag into wait-for-tick (uniform on [0,300) s) plus 49–79 s of job runtime, both ours. What these events genuinely buy is the asynchronous outcome our synchronous `createPost` cannot see, plus an inbox that survives Zernio being down. **MUTATION TESTING FOUND DEAD CODE WEARING A CONFIDENT COMMENT.** `upsertThread` carried an `updated_at = case when (…) is distinct from (…) then now() else … end` whose comment claimed it was "what makes nothing changed true" — and replacing the entire expression with a bare `now()` changed NO test result, because `inbox_foundations.sql:161` puts a BEFORE UPDATE trigger on the table and `app.set_updated_at` assigns `now()` unconditionally, overwriting whatever the statement wrote. It was decoration asserting a guarantee it did not provide. Deleted rather than kept as harmless, and replaced by a test that pins the trigger from both sides; the real mechanism is one thing, the duplicate short-circuit, and on a replay `upsertThread` is never reached at all. **A GUARD FAILED FOR THE RIGHT REASON AND REPORTED THE WRONG ONE.** `middleware.test.ts` matched `/'([^']+)'/g` over raw source; every entry in that block is preceded by a comment, so the apostrophe in "Zernio's inbound events" opened a quote that closed on the next apostrophe and invented two routes made of prose. `lib/cron/wiring.test.ts` learned this on 2026-08-19 **on the same block of source** and this file never got the fix — the fix belongs to the parser, not to the comment, or the next person rediscovers it. Meanwhile `wiring.test.ts` itself caught the new public route THROUGH a ten-line comment, which demonstrates its 2026-08-19 strip rather than asserting it, and `turbo-env-wiring` caught `ZERNIO_WEBHOOK_SECRET` missing from `@sahoda/web#build.env` — under `envMode: strict` that is stripped from the build, so the endpoint would have answered `503 not_configured` forever with the value correctly set in Vercel. **AND A FIXTURE FIELD NEARLY COST REAL EVENTS**: a `webhook.test` payload carrying `timestamp: "t"` raised `invalid input syntax for type timestamp with time zone` on insert, the handler correctly answered 503 — and a 503 makes Zernio RETRY, seven times over 51 hours, each attempt re-raising the same error, then dead-letter a genuine delivery over a decorative field. Unparseable stamps are now NULL; the raw value is still in the stored payload. **ONE MORE CORRECTION TO MY OWN NOTE**: I wrote that nothing in this schema stores Zernio's post id. `apps/jobs/src/reconcile/store.ts:128` records that `post_publish_logs.platform_post_id` holds ZERNIO's id on the STILL_PROCESSING path — "a different column, and a different meaning, from `post_variants.platform_post_id`". True of `posts` and `post_variants`, not true absolutely. And the reconciliation sweep here does NOT duplicate `apps/jobs/src/reconcile/sweep.ts`, which already runs and is already correct; it covers the set that one structurally cannot see, because `listUnresolvedPublishes` requires a log row carrying an id and `publish/store.ts:173` documents the hole where a post is live with no log row and gets published AGAIN.

# LEARNINGS — SAHODA LABS

2026-08-22 · packages/db + packages/research + apps/web (wt-knowledge) — **A guard that cannot fire is worse than no guard, and this run produced four of them; every one was found by breaking something on purpose.** `apply_tenant_read_policy` creates ONE policy, `t_select`, so a read-only table refuses writes in TWO shapes: an INSERT raises `violates row-level security policy`, and an UPDATE or DELETE finds no visible row and **succeeds having changed nothing**. The first version of the test read that vacuous success as a write being permitted and reported four working guards as broken; the honest assertion is `affectedRows === 0` AND the row reading back unchanged. PostgreSQL's one-argument `btrim(text)` strips **spaces only** — not tabs, not newlines — so `where btrim(chunk) <> ''` stored a passage of `E'\n'` and the library gained a search hit containing nothing; four call sites now ask `~ '[^[:space:]]'`, which is what "not blank" actually means. `revoke all on function … from public` removes the PUBLIC pseudo-role's grant and leaves the one Supabase's default privileges hand DIRECTLY to `anon` — measured immediately after applying: `resolve_brand_memory`, `resolve_memory_event` and `upsert_connection` all deny anon; all five new functions allowed it, and the revoke had reported success. And the one that never shipped: a citation written as `document:<uuid>#<ordinal>` stops matching `delete_knowledge_document`'s exact `= 'document:' || id`, so the delete gate would have kept running, reported `brand_fields: 0` about a document every field came from, and let the delete through without the acknowledgement it exists to demand. Twenty-two mutants, twenty-two killed; the two that mattered most were _over_-widening (a bare `like 'document:%'` matches other documents) and _under_-widening, so both directions are asserted. **`security_invoker` is the quietest way to lose tenant isolation, and the whole isolation suite could not see it.** `knowledge_current_chunks` is the schema's first `public` VIEW carrying a `workspace_id`; removing `with (security_invoker = true)` let workspace B read workspace A's passages while every policy underneath still read correctly in the migration. `rls_tenant_isolation.pglite.test.ts` is catalog-driven and covered the two new TABLES automatically (measured: both seeded, 2 rows each, 0 unseeded) — and `tenantTables()` read `information_schema.columns`, which lists views, so it tried to INSERT into the view and called a covered table unseeded. Narrowed to `BASE TABLE`, plus a new catalog-driven check that every workspace-scoped view declares `security_invoker`. **A `'use client'` module importing a `'server-only'` one returns HTTP 500 for EVERY ROUTE IN THE APP, and passes tsc, eslint, design-lint and 3,705 unit tests.** One number — an upload cap — reached across the line; the only thing that found it was loading a page, and it presented as three Playwright specs failing with `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`, which reads as a dead server. The new guard checks VALUE imports only: `import type` is erased, ten shipped files do it correctly, and a version that flagged both would be red on day one and switched off in its first week. **The per-field-evidence impossibility was true of one path and had become half wrong.** `brain-origin.ts` argues that nothing links a Brand Brain field to a passage — correct for `brand_guidelines`, which reads everything in one pass. `brand_extract` already had the answer: it cites a BLOCK INDEX, `attachProvenance` resolves it against a list we built, and an index we did not supply drops the field. The model emits a number; the document id is resolved by code the model cannot reach. So the injection proof needs no model call and no detector — a detector printing "0 findings" would offer safety that `quarantine.ts` already measured as unavailable (twelve published defences bypassed at >90% by a 2025 joint OpenAI/Anthropic/Google DeepMind team). Six structural properties instead, including the uncomfortable one: the field the injection ACTUALLY WON is not dropped, because an obeyed instruction is indistinguishable from an honest reading — it simply arrives `confirmed: false`, citing a uuid we chose. `neutralize` now RETURNS what it rewrote, so the count on screen is produced by the code that did the rewriting and cannot drift from it. **And one defect that was already live: `resolve_memory_event` writes `source = 'system'`, which is also what the model-unreachable fallback writes, so accepting a Loop learning made /brain/resolve tell the owner their entire brand is "a sample, not your brand… not answers about your business".** Measured against production on a throwaway workspace. Fixed by reading `memory_events.applied_memory_version` — a stored fact, not an inference — rather than moving a frozen contract to fix a rendering bug.
2026-08-21 · apps/web + packages/db + packages/shared (wt-media) — **A derivative that is its own `assets` row opens the exact data-loss door the delete gate was built to shut, and the door is opened by a SCHEMA choice rather than by any line of code.** The gate is `app.assert_asset_not_in_use()`, which reads `asset_usages`, which a trigger derives from `post_media.asset_id` — so it can only ever see an attachment that NAMES ITS ORIGINAL. Had a cropped copy been a second `assets` row with `post_media.asset_id` pointing at the crop, deleting the original would have found zero usages, the trigger would have refused nothing, and a post scheduled for Thursday would have lost its photo silently. So `asset_derivatives` is a CHILD table cascading from `assets`, `post_media.asset_id` keeps naming the original and a new `derivative_id` says which crop is attached, and two constraints make the bypass unwritable rather than merely unwritten: `check (derivative_id is null or asset_id is not null)` and a composite FK `(derivative_id, asset_id) → asset_derivatives (id, asset_id)`. All three guards were MUTATED and all three killed a test. **The offer had to be built so that the refusal could not change, and the shape that guarantees it is "beside", not "instead of".** `decideAttach` returns exactly what it returned, both attach actions still refuse with the same sentence and still write nothing, and the offer is an extra field on the same `ok:false`. Declining therefore needed no code — it is the ABSENCE of the accept call — and five component tests pin it, including an accept test so that a build where the accept button was also inert could not pass the other four. **Composing that offer could destroy the refusal it rode on, and an existing test written for something else found it.** `offerForAsset` reaches storage; it runs inside the refusal arm AFTER the action has composed a sentence and a per-channel objection list, so a throw escaped to that action's catch and replaced both with "Could not add that file — try again". `assets.test.ts`'s "the refusal names the channel rather than failing anonymously" went red the moment the call was added. Both offer composers are now total. **The Constraint Engine declares no canonical size for any channel — only floors and bands — and LinkedIn declares nothing at all.** `imageDims` is `{minW, minH, aspectRange?}`. Writing "1080×1080 for a feed post, 1200×628 for LinkedIn" would be inventing the very thing the target table exists to read, so the crop is cut to the NEAREST EDGE of the band every selected channel can live inside at once, and LinkedIn's row on screen says it states no rule. Only Instagram declares a band, so the intersection is never empty today; `'empty'` is still a real return value. **The rounding at the band edge is the whole geometry problem.** `validateMedia` compares with a plain `<` and `>` and Instagram's floor of 0.75 was measured at the boundary, so `round(999 × 0.75) = 749` and `749/999 = 0.74975` — a crop cut, stored, attached, and then refused by the same engine that asked for it. `fitInBand` verifies every candidate against that identical comparison and returns only one that passes. Four mutants die, including the two axes of the placement clamp SEPARATELY: the first suite's x-clamp mutant survived because every case happened to clamp to itself. **Three UI defects that typecheck, the design lint and every test were all blind to, found only by measuring in Chromium at four widths.** `Modal` had no internal scroll, so long content grew past the UA's own `max-height` for `<dialog>`, the overflow was CLIPPED rather than scrolled, and BOTH footer buttons were off-screen — Escape still worked, so the only reachable answer was the one that does nothing, on a dialog whose entire purpose is a decision. `max-h-[calc(100dvh-2rem)]` is INVALID CSS, because `calc()` requires whitespace around a minus, so the browser dropped the declaration while the class sat in the markup and in the compiled stylesheet looking exactly like a fix (Tailwind spells that space `_`). And a `max-h` on a `w-full` box clamps the height and leaves the width alone, drawing the crop at the WRONG ASPECT — width must be derived from the height cap and the ratio. **Sharp's `extract` names its origin `left`/`top`, not `x`/`y`, and a `CropRect` passed straight in TYPE-CHECKS** because every field is optional in sharp's typings; nine tests failed at once. It also reports `cropOffsetTop` as a NEGATIVE number, and a same-aspect resize is not a crop at all — which is what an earlier version of the subject-finder tests was accidentally measuring. **Most photographs need no crop.** MEASURED: 4032×3024 and 3024×4032 are 1.33 and 0.75, both inside Instagram's band, so the offer fires only for 9:16 and for panoramas — 3 of 20 representative cases. A cropped JPEG derivative came out at 0.09× its original (a smaller region at q82 mozjpeg against a q88 full-frame), so the multiplier is ~1.09× per cropped photo and ~1.01× across all cases; a cropped PNG re-encoded as PNG is the worst case at ~1.96×, because lossless stays lossless.

Found by Postgres REJECTING an insert of `'connected'`, not by reading the code — the two correct call sites (`lib/connections/read.ts:100`, `lib/audience/page-data.ts:148`) had been sitting beside the two wrong ones the whole time. Left for the Loop's lane with a REQUESTS entry, because flipping it turns a permanently-empty list into a populated one. **SOLID vs HATCHED, MEASURED BY COMPOSITED LUMINANCE:** `.is-real` 308 against `.is-simulated` 1000 in light and 3 in dark (Δ692 / Δ305), separated by fill weight in BOTH themes before texture is counted — matching docs/26 §3.1 exactly. Comparing colour strings would have passed on hue, the one channel not allowed to be load-bearing. **AND TWO LAYOUT FACTS.** A grid item's default `min-width: auto` refuses to shrink below its content's min-content width, so `truncate` on the watch-list name never fired and the page measured 464px at a 390 viewport — found by an overflow probe naming the three offending `li`s, not by looking. 390 and 1440 both land in terminal bands of a two-breakpoint app (700 / 1180) and neither exercises 700–1179, so the camera shoots 1024 too. **THE FIXTURE STORE WAS PER-REQUEST**, so every add and remove closed over a `snap` discarded before the next render — it would have surfaced as a broken server action at screenshot time; memoised at module scope, with the memo caching the STORE and never the decision, and the production refusal asserted by really setting `NODE_ENV=production`. **THE SUPABASE BINDING GUESSED ANOTHER LANE'S COLUMNS AND RETURNED A 500.** The first `store.ts` selected `id, name, url, kind, added_on, last_observed_at` from `competitors`, with the names taken from TSD §Radar/Playbooks — which names TABLES and not their columns. The table is REAL, because the parallel wt-radar lane applied its migrations to the shared database, so the missing-table branch never fired: Postgres answered 42703 (undefined column), not 42P01, the throw reached the server component, and every `/radar` request 500'd. It cost `roadmap-honesty` (no "not built yet" text, because the screen rendered nothing at all) and `every-section-loads` (no `h1`). A speculative query is a dependency on the branch you were told not to depend on, and a worse one than an import — invisible until it runs, and fatal. The binding now reads nothing and `store.test.ts` scans the FILE for a query surface, because returning UNWIRED from inside a `try/catch` would satisfy a behavioural assertion while restoring the crash. **THE SMOKE LEG IS TWO DIFFERENT SUITES DEPENDING ON THE SERVER, AND THE GATE ONLY EVER RUNS ONE.** Against `pnpm dev` the run died twice at `concurrent-edit.spec.ts` — once with a kernel OOM kill of `next-server` (2.3GB RSS, 02:28:46) and once with no OOM at all — producing 78 `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` and ~70 "failures" that are one event plus its echoes. Against `next start` (`E2E_SERVER_CMD`, which `playwright.config.ts` documents) the same tree ran 88/89 with zero refusals. The one real failure only exists in the built CSS: `motion.spec.ts`'s scrim check reads `--scrim` off `documentElement` and parses the alpha with `/[\d.]+\s*\)$/`, but Next's minifier rewrites `rgb(0 0 0 / .4)` to `#0006` — same 0.4, hex-encoded, no parenthesis — so the regex misses and `?? '1'` turns "I could not parse this" into "the value is 1". The BACKDROP measured 0.4 and was correct all along; the test manufactured the wrong expectation, and the gate cannot see it because dev does not minify. `motion.spec.ts`, `modal.tsx`, `drawer.tsx`, `globals.css` and `tokens.css` are byte-identical to the base branch, which is how it was established the failure was not this lane's. `/radar` STAYS on `roadmap-honesty`'s list and its allowance got NARROWER (1–5 slots gone, price only): `PlanLimits` defines no competitor dimension and the docs that mention a cap disagree — PRD §7.1 says 3, PRD/FSD M9 say 1–5 — so drawing either was picking an entitlement rather than reading one.
2026-08-21 · packages/db + apps/web (wt-remix) — **A guard that skips is a guard that has never run, and the way to find out what it was hiding is to write the version that cannot skip.** `export-drift.test.ts` exists to fail when a workspace-owned table is missing from the DPDP export manifest, and it is `describe.skip` without `SUPABASE_DB_URL` — which is never set, correctly, because the only Supabase project this repo has IS production and `forbidden-target.ts` refuses it by ref. Rewriting the same two questions against the MIGRATION FILES on PGlite, in process, with no credentials, found **seven tables missing from every export**: `audience_snapshots`, `billing_profiles`, `invoices` and the Loop's four. Four of those are the record of what a customer was charged for and what Sahoda decided on their behalf, and the export was still headed "everything you own" — the one claim it must never make falsely, because the person reading it cannot check. The manifest's own header said "MEASURED against production on 2026-08-19: 30 tables"; it was 39. The same check applied to `your-data-panel.tsx` found its deletion argument resting on "15 of 30 tables have no member DELETE policy" when it is 20 of 39. **A number in a comment is a claim nobody checks**, so both now come out of `pg_policies` on every gate run (`export_manifest.pglite.test.ts`, `deletion_reach.pglite.test.ts`) — and the second deliberately asserts the PROPERTY rather than the count, because pinning 20 would fail on every migration and train people to edit the number instead of thinking about it. **Two doors into a table are two different trust problems and they do not get the same shape.** `leads` had RLS and no writer since July. The public one takes a site SLUG and NO workspace id — a service-role function that accepted one would be cross-tenant lead injection from a public endpoint — and it is service_role only, because `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` ships to every browser and 20260727072107 already ruled that granting such a function to `anon` IS the open-insert path it was written to avoid. The member one takes a workspace id and checks it against `app.member_workspace_ids()` INSIDE, written as `exists` and never `not in`: `x not in (subquery)` evaluates to NULL the moment the subquery yields one, `if NULL` takes the else branch, and the guard waves the row through. Both are `SECURITY DEFINER` functions rather than policies, which is what keeps the two assertions `rls.test.ts` has pinned since July — a member cannot INSERT or DELETE a lead even in their own tenant — TRUE after the migration rather than edited to suit it. **And a door onto a table nothing writes is a door onto a wall**: `lead_from_inbox` derives every field from an `inbox_threads` row and `inbox_threads` ships deliberately empty, so the button a person can actually press calls a sibling that takes the details from the live Zernio screen and records `source.details = 'from_client'` — because a lead somebody typed in and a lead Sahoda observed are different facts and the row has to say which. **A refusal test that asserts `ok:false` proves nothing about the money.** Remix's halt is `approved_at`, a stored fact, so it can be tested by forcing `status='running'` with no approval and asserting `withCredits` WAS NEVER CALLED — the ledger, not the sentence. MEASURED: swapping that gate for a `status === 'planned'` check failed only the two assertions that pin the MESSAGE, because a batch with no approval also has no approved total and the price re-check refuses it too; the four that asserted `ok:false` alone stayed green. Two guards standing on one hole look exactly like one guard working. **And one kind is one model call, so one kind is one charge**: none of the frozen mesh tasks takes a channel — `caption_rewrite`'s input is `{text, instruction, selection?}` and has no channel field at all — so four channels of "a short version" is ONE call, pricing it per channel would quote four times the real figure, and the screen has to SAY that unticking a channel takes away a draft and not a credit rather than leave a person to infer it from a number that did not move.

2026-08-20 · packages/db + apps/jobs + apps/web (wt-audience) — **The platform does not tell you it is withholding data; it hands you a 200 with empty arrays, which is byte-identical to "there is nothing here".** `/v1/analytics/instagram/demographics` answered `{success:true, demographics:{age:[],city:[],country:[],gender:[]}}` for the workspace's real connected account (1 follower), MEASURED live 2026-08-20. Zernio's own OpenAPI documents a 400 with `code:"instagram_insufficient_followers"` for exactly this case and it DID NOT FIRE; Meta states the rule in the passive voice — "Not returned if the IG User has less than 100 followers" — and "not returned" is literally what arrives. So suppression is not something the read reports, it is something the SCREEN infers, and it may only be inferred with a follower count in hand. `audience-state.ts` refuses to conclude it otherwise: `classifyAudience` with `followers: null` returns `no-data`, never `suppressed`, and a THROWN call never becomes `suppressed` whatever the follower count is. That ordering is why the collector reads the follower series FIRST and why `follower_count` lives in `audience_snapshots` rather than a table of its own — without it, an empty demographics result is indistinguishable from a failure and the screen has to guess between "you are fine, you are just small" and "something is broken", which is the defect class `e2e/no-impossible-remedy.spec.ts` exists for and that this product has shipped four times. **The sibling table's best idea is wrong here, and both divergences are tested rather than argued.** `post_metric_snapshots.measured_on` is GENERATED from `measured_at` so the two can never disagree; `audience_snapshots.measured_on` is SUPPLIED, because the demographics endpoint returns no timestamp of any kind and the follower endpoint returns ~30 DATED points in one response — generating the day from `now()` would stack all thirty onto today and lose twenty-nine days that cannot be re-fetched. And the account id carries NO foreign key to `connections`, because a disconnect-and-reconnect would cascade away history no platform will ever hand back. **A mutation battery found the gap a full suite missed, twice, and both times it was a test that could not fail.** On the migration, 7 of 8 mutants died and the survivor was _adding a default_ to `measured_on` — every test supplied the column, so the default was never reached; the fix was not a stronger constraint but an assertion that the column is genuinely required. On the greyscale proof, mutant "give the inferred layer the measured layer's solid fill" passed, because the probe sampled each panel at its CENTRE and the centre lands on the panel's own body TEXT — it was measuring ink, not fill. A median over a run inside the padding band kills it. That same run also recorded a fact worth keeping: `bg-brand` does NOT override `.is-proposed`'s `background: transparent` in this build (the pixels came out byte-identical), so a Tailwind utility can silently do nothing on a certainty-rung element. **Two defects in existing code, both found by rendering rather than reading.** `<Button asChild>` throws `Slot failed to slot onto its children` — Button unconditionally renders a loading-spinner slot beside `{children}`, so Radix's `Slot` receives two; `button.tsx` documents this in its own header and nothing enforced it, and `buttonVariants` on a `<Link>` is the route. And `bg-s2` resolves to `--surface-2`, which in DARK is the same value as `--surface` — any progress track built on it is invisible there, the same "a tint cannot be a signature" trap docs/26 §3.1 records for `.is-committed`. **The brief's own list was wrong on one item and the OpenAPI settled it.** "Online hours" does not exist in Zernio: `online_followers` appears NOWHERE in the 2.29 MB, 397-path document. Meta still documents the metric; our only integration path does not expose it. The two endpoints that look like substitutes are not — `/analytics/inbox/heatmap` measures when people MESSAGED you and `/analytics/best-time` measures your own posts' engagement, different populations and different claims. **HATCHED was asked for and DASHED was shipped, on purpose.** `.is-simulated` (hatch) means NOT REAL — a fixture that never touched a platform — and stamping an inference drawn from real measurements with it would destroy the one mark that tells a customer a number never left the building. `.is-proposed` is this app's existing word for "Sahoda suggests it; nobody agreed". Separation MEASURED from composited PNG pixels, not colour strings: light 212 vs 1000, dark 212 vs 3, dashed-edge spread 485 and 64.
2026-08-20 · The Loop, M2 (wt-loop) — **A guard that has only ever been tested against the state it was written for shares the blind spot of the code it guards; the live database is what finds the third state.** `loop_kill_switch` cancels live cycles, then unschedules "the posts of cancelled cycles" — and separately, and correctly, refuses to cancel a cycle already `reported`, because rewriting a finished week is a lie. Both halves defensible, and together they left the commonest case on the floor: a cycle that FINISHED PLANNING keeps its posts on the calendar for days, so "the Loop planned my week and I want it stopped" did nothing at all. Forty-one PGlite tests passed throughout, because every one of them cancelled a LIVE cycle — the fixture and the defect had the same shape. It surfaced only by running a real cycle to completion against production and then asking the database `status IN ('approved','scheduled') AND scheduled_at <= now()`: seven rows, four of them mine. The fix separates the two scopes (cycles: live only; posts: every Loop post whatever its cycle's state; briefs: still cancelled-only, because rewriting a reported brief to 'skipped' would make that week's CMO Report describe work that did happen as work that did not), and the new test fails against the old function with `expected +0 to be 1`. Two smaller versions of the same lesson in one session: a structural guard asserting `reflect.ts` imports no mesh FAILED ON ITS OWN DOC COMMENT (which explains at length that there is no mesh, so it contains every word a naive scan flags), and the repair then lower-cased the haystack while comparing against `'runTask'` — so `'runtask'.includes('runTask')` was false for every input and an injected model call walked straight through a guard that could never fire. **A guard is not a guard until you have watched it go red.**

2026-08-20 · The Loop, M2 (wt-loop) — **An optional parameter that changes what a prompt CONTAINS is not optional, and omitting it wrote four dispatch-eligible posts into production.** `plan-week.ts`'s `buildMessages` drops the "Today is …" line ENTIRELY when `nowIso` is absent — no default, no warning, just a model with no idea what "the coming week" means. The server action always passed it; the live harness I wrote to exercise "what the action does" did not, and the model returned slots in **June 2025**, fourteen months in the past. Those became posts with `status='approved'` and a past `scheduled_at`, which is exactly the dispatcher's gate — only `SAHODA_PUBLISH_ENABLED` being absent from both env files stood between that and a real Instagram post. Three things worth carrying. A harness that claims to do what an action does has to actually do it, or it proves a copy and hides the divergence in the gap. The assertion that would have caught it costs one line (`every brief's slot must be in the future`) and did not exist because past dates were not a failure mode anyone had imagined. And the failure surfaced as `PROVIDER_ERROR` from `withCredits` while `ai_provider_logs` recorded the same call `status='ok'` — **when a wrapper reports a provider error, read the provider's own log before believing it**; the throw was in my INSERT, and the provider was blameless. (That insert was its own lesson: columns from a bare `VALUES` list have no declared type, so `v.workspace_id` arrives as text and a `uuid` column refuses it. A plain multi-row `insert ... values` takes its types from the target columns.)

2026-08-20 · The Loop, M2 (wt-loop) — **"Produced by a query" is not the same as "true enough to tell someone", and arithmetic can fabricate an insight just as fluently as a model.** The Reflect stage proposes learnings written back to the Brand Brain, so its output is a claim about the customer's own business — the one class of statement this product may never invent. The obvious defence is a prompt instruction, and it is worthless: the test for it asserts on output copy, which a prompt-instructed model passes while inventing freely. So Reflect calls NO MODEL AT ALL — it is division, and a test reads the source to prove the module imports no mesh and cannot reach a provider. That moves the whole problem to the evidence floor, and the live data showed why the floor is the actual work: production holds, for the only workspace with any history, **five Instagram posts at 1–3 impressions and exactly ONE LinkedIn post at 63**. Mean over mean says LinkedIn beats Instagram thirty-one fold; every figure traceable to a real row; the sentence is worthless. Four gates now stand in the way (three posts per arm, two arms, a leader mean of at least 10, a lift of at least 25%), that exact live shape is a test case asserting nothing is emitted, and — the part that matters for copy — "nothing was measured" and "measured, but too thin" are returned as DIFFERENT reasons, because only one of them is an admission that the product has nothing yet.
2026-08-20 · repo-wide (wt-hollow) — **A suite that runs nothing reports the same green as a suite that passes, and this repo was shipping 251 of them.** `vitest --reporter=json` per package, before touching anything: 6,420 passed, 0 failed, **251 SKIPPED across twenty files that executed zero tests**. `packages/db` skipped more than it ran (201 vs 202). The skips were never the mistake — the only Postgres this repo points at IS production, and these suites ran against it on 2026-07-27, which is why the gate exists. Having no other database was the mistake. **The unlock was three statements.** `pglite-schema.ts` states that RLS "must not" be claimed enforced because PGlite connects as superuser; that is true of the DEFAULT connection and false of what it can be made to do — `begin; set local role authenticated;` drops `is_superuser` to `off` and Postgres applies the policy exactly as it does behind PostgREST. All 44 migrations boot on PGlite (48 tables, 89 policies, 30 with `workspace_id`), so RLS, the 26 billing integration tests and the 16 jobs ones now run on every gate with no credentials and no network. **THE TRAP THAT MAKES THIS DANGEROUS RATHER THAN MERELY HARD: a bare Postgres grants `authenticated` nothing, so every read returns `permission denied` — and a tenant-isolation suite written on that box reports FLAWLESS isolation while proving only that nobody had been granted anything.** Supabase grants `anon` and `authenticated` full CRUD on `public` at project creation precisely because in its model the GRANT is not the boundary; RLS is. Reproducing the BROAD grant is what makes the test mean anything, and narrowing it would have made the suite look stronger and be worthless. Found the hard way: `ai_provider_logs` denied its own owner. **TWO DEFECTS IN MY OWN HARNESS, both caught by mutation and neither visible in review.** `seedTwoWorkspaces` ran `disable trigger all` to insert and never put them back, so every later assertion ran against a database with `app.block_mutations()` globally disarmed — a harness telling its own tests what to conclude. And that disarming was HIDING a real gap: no executing test covered the ledger's append-only guarantee at all (`post_metric_snapshots.pglite.test.ts` covers one table; `ledger.test.ts` covers `credit_ledger` and is `describe.skipIf`, so it has never run), and dropping the trigger left every test in apps/jobs and packages/billing green. **THE TECHNIQUE THAT FOUND THE VACUOUS GUARDS IS WORTH KEEPING: re-point a guard's corpus at a directory that EXISTS but holds none of the files it scans** (`ops/state` — four .json, no .ts). A throw would be red for the wrong reason; an empty-but-valid walk is the exact ambiguity. Eight guards tested, six sound, two passing on an empty source tree — including `breakpoints.test.ts`, which guards the dead `sm:`/`md:`/`lg:` prefixes, the most expensive defect this design system has had. **`lint` was `exit 0` in all nine packages**, so one of the gate's three verbs could not fail on any input; replaced with five rules aimed at exactly these failure modes (`test-only`, `assertionless-test`, `stale-exception`, `uncollected-tests`, `console-log`), ratcheted per package so three live peers do not go red, and each proven red by planting its violation. `uncollected-tests` closes a hole in `test-collection.test.ts`, which only counts tests under `src/` and therefore cannot see packages/db's 22 files under `tests/`. **MEASURED: adding the linter took turbo from `9 cached` to `0 cached` only after `scripts/lint.mjs` joined `globalDependencies` — a root-file rules change is invisible to every package hash and would have replayed a stale FULL TURBO pass.** **FOUR TIMES I WAS WRONG AND THE MEASUREMENT SAID SO.** (1) `assets.spec.ts`'s seven untagged tests read as the largest finding and are not one — the file states in its own header that it uploads real bytes to storage and is deliberately outside `--grep @smoke`; a documented exclusion is the opposite of a hollow test. (2) Three attempts at the `assertionless-test` mutant SURVIVED, and each time the MUTANT was wrong rather than the rule — it counts the literal text `expect(`, so aliasing or shadowing the binding leaves every call site intact. (3) I predicted the @smoke wall clock was Turbopack cold compiles; compiles are **45 s of 1219 s**, and the real figure is **861 s of dev-server response time, 607 s of it on WARM routes** — a different browser waits exactly as long, so Lightpanda cannot move it and a production build can. (4) I judged a Lightpanda screenshot "plausibly rendered" because 10,704 bytes cleared a size threshold; `/sign-in`, `/sign-up` and `about:blank` produce ONE distinct SHA-256 between them. It is a placeholder, and a "bigger than 3 KB" gate would pass on every page of the app forever. **Lightpanda, verified on this machine rather than from documentation:** CDP, `Page.navigate` and `document.title` work; `Target.createBrowserContext` is REFUSED outright ("Cannot have more than one browser context at a time"), which is why Playwright's `connectOverCDP` hangs; `getComputedStyle` returns `""`. The blocker is JavaScript, not pixels — the client-rendered sign-in page reaches 1 anchor / 3 buttons / 2 inputs after eight seconds, so the "does this element exist, what does it say" checks the brief proposes to move are precisely the ones that do not materialise. **And `shell-probe.spec.ts`, made to assert and tagged, went red on its first run** with two real controls under the 44px touch floor at 390×844: `Create workspace` at 164×34 and `Sahoda — go to Home` at 26×44. It had been `test.skip` unless `DESIGN_AUDIT=1`, carried no `@smoke` tag, and asserted nothing — three conditions had to hold for it to be worth anything and none did.

2026-08-20 · apps/web (wt-ia) — **A screen nobody links to is indistinguishable from a screen that does not exist, and this app had three of them shipped.** `/approvals`, `/campaigns` and `/assets` were finished, tested, deployed features reachable only by typing the URL; on a phone it was worse, because the bottom bar's four tabs were the COMPLETE map below 700px and seventeen of twenty-one sections had no door at all. Nothing caught it and nothing could: a route with no link is not a type error, not a lint error, and not a failing test. The fix is one map (`lib/nav/sections.ts`) projected by the rail, a new More sheet and the command palette — the palette used to carry a comment explaining that it omitted `/sites` "for the same reason the rail does", which is a comment that exists only because two lists can disagree — plus `lib/nav/reachable.test.ts`, which fails on any top-level route under `(app)` that is neither in the map nor declared with the other way it is reached. **Four things a REAL BROWSER found that reading the source did not.** (1) `md:` `sm:` `lg:` compile to NOTHING here — `globals.css` wipes the stock scale with `--breakpoint-*: initial` and Tailwind does not warn about an unknown variant, so `grid md:grid-cols-2` was a one-column grid at every width on fifteen classes across thirteen files, five of them on the Ads section whose entire job is to show what a screen will look like. (2) The rail hid 220px of itself: 938px of nav in a 718px box with `offsetWidth - clientWidth === 0`, because Linux Chromium paints an overlay scrollbar that takes no layout width and does not appear until you already know to scroll — `scrollbar-width: thin`, that property scoped to non-webkit, and a forced `::-webkit-scrollbar` width each measured 0, and the fix that works is a MASK (`.scroll-fade`) because a fading edge reads as "this continues". (3) The rail rendered `100 of —`, the exact string docs/26 §4 names as the reason there is no absence class for "does not exist" — `rail-foot.tsx` reasoned the denominator out of existence in its own docstring, correctly omitted the ratio BAR for that reason, and printed the words anyway. (4) A bottom `<Drawer>` can never be full width without `max-w-none`, because the UA stylesheet caps every `dialog` at `calc((100% - 6px) - 2em)` in both axes — measured at 352px on a 390px phone — and `max-h-none` sitting in the shared half of the class string beat `max-h-[80dvh]` in the side half, since one property at equal specificity is decided by Tailwind's emitted order and not by the order written. **A merged lane can bring a spec written against a tree you do not have.** `campaigns.spec.ts` waited 90 seconds on `[data-composer]`, twice, at 1.8 minutes each — and that selector, with `[data-version-card]`, exists ONLY in `components/composer/` on `wt-composer` and `wt-editor2`, neither ever merged into this lineage. Both its @smoke tests could not pass here and were not flaky. Repointed at the sequence `golden-path.spec.ts` proves in the same run, with a note saying the OLD helper is correct for the tree that has the composer rewrite. **And one broken screen inside `(app)` takes down every test in the repo while hiding itself**: Next tries to render `(app)/error.tsx`, the dev server answers `Could not find the module …#default in the React Client Manifest`, stops serving, and 59 tests fail on `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` without one of them naming the page. `every-section-loads.spec.ts` walks all twenty-one destinations and asserts each renders its own `h1` — by name, because an error boundary renders with a 200 and so does a blank page.

2026-08-19 · apps/web + packages/shared (wt-camp) — **A placeholder screen can carry a word the database has never accepted, and the bug it creates is invisible because it wears the costume of a correct empty state.** `/campaigns` stood for weeks as a drawing: three fictional cards, an em dash in every figure slot, and a comment saying there was no campaigns table. There was one — `campaigns` and `campaign_posts` were applied to production with RLS and four policies each (VERIFIED by a read-only `psql` probe: both tables live, `relrowsecurity = t`, 8 policies, 0 rows) and nothing read them. Its filter chips read `All · Active · Draft · Completed`. Three of those are values the column accepts. The fourth is not: the check constraint says `finished`. Nobody would ever have reported it, because a filter built from that label matches zero rows forever and zero rows is exactly what a customer with no finished campaigns also sees. THE FIX IS NOT A CAREFUL READING, IT IS A TEST THAT EXECUTES THE BAD SPELLING. `packages/shared/src/db/campaigns.status-values.test.ts` feeds `CampaignSchema` the literal string `Completed`, prints the refusal (`refused: Invalid option: expected one of "draft"|"active"|"finished"|"cancelled"`), and asserts the four accepted values parse to themselves — so a guard nobody has watched fail is not what ships. The same file asserts the CONTRACT HAS NO KEY for `budget`, `spend`, `roas`, `reach`, `conversions` or `health`, which is the reference design's entire card: a nullable field would put those words in the type, a word in a type becomes a slot on a screen, and a slot on a screen gets filled with a dash. Absent beats nullable when there is no source. TWO MORE DEFECTS CAME OUT OF GUARDS RATHER THAN REVIEW, AND BOTH WERE IN CODE I HAD JUST WRITTEN AND READ. (1) Playwright strict mode found TWO primary "Create campaign" buttons on the empty screen — the header rendered one on `status === 'ok'` and the empty state rendered another, which is docs/26 §1.5's "two primary actions means neither is the main action" arriving from the two arms of one conditional rather than from carelessness; the fix is that the header button renders only when there IS a list. (2) An Ads guard asserting no `data-inert-control` may contain a digit failed on `Last 7 days`, and the guard was right twice over — the label carries a figure AND a seven-day window implies seven days of something on a screen with nothing behind it. Now `This week`. THE THIRD FAILURE WAS MY OWN TEST BEING WRONG, WHICH IS WORTH THE SAME HONESTY. An assertion that the grid contains no em dash anywhere failed on `— still being written`, the status chip's screen-reader hint. That is prose, and the repo's standing ruling is that em dashes stay and nothing runs a bulk strip. The rule docs/26 §4 actually states is narrower — a dash may not be rendered IN PLACE OF a missing value — so the assertion now checks each `<td>` for content that IS a bare dash. A ban on a character and a ban on a claim are not the same guard, and only one of them is the rule. FINALLY, THE GRID FORCED A GAP IN docs/26 ITSELF, now closed there: §4 says a quantity that does not exist gets its slot deleted, but a matrix emits a `<td>` at every intersection whether or not the quantity exists, so "delete the slot" is impossible. The answer is render no mark and carry one `sr-only` sentence — the visible answer is still nothing, and a silent cell would make the absence invisible rather than legible. Also added to §10.1: a matrix is not `DataTable`, because `DataTable` renders every body cell as `<td>` and a matrix's row label must be `<th scope="row">` or a screen reader announces the column without the record.
2026-08-20 · assets (wt-assets) — **A "used in" read is only a delete gate if the DATABASE holds it, and a usage table nothing writes is a gate with no evidence.** `assets` and `asset_usages` were live in production with RLS and four policies each, and nothing read or wrote either — so `delete from assets` SUCCEEDED, cascaded the usage rows away, and stripped the photo off a scheduled post with nobody told. The fix people reach for is a check in the server action, and it is not enough twice over: it is TOCTOU (a post can be scheduled between the read and the delete), and it covers exactly one caller. The refusal went into `before delete on assets` as `app.assert_asset_not_in_use`, raising `restrict_violation` with the post's TITLE in the message; the server action's job is now to produce a SENTENCE, not to be the guard. Proved by attempting the delete for real — 26/26 live checks, anon key + minted member JWTs — and the message that came back is `This file is used by a post that cannot lose it: "Diwali offer" (scheduled to go out). Remove it from that post first, or keep the file.` **Three things the build turned up that were not visible from the schema.** (1) `apps/jobs/src/publish/store.ts` reads ONE table for a post's photos, `post_media`, so writing an `asset_usages` row and nothing else would have shipped an attached photo that never goes out — a success message over a publish without its picture. Attaching therefore still writes `post_media`, plus a nullable `asset_id` pointing back at the library; `docs/30` records that ruling and the three alternatives rejected. (2) The library attachment REUSES the library's storage object rather than copying it, which is the whole economy of a library — and `detachMedia` deleted the object unconditionally, so taking one photo off one draft would have deleted it from the library and from every other post using it. Same data loss, other door. (3) `20260819000400`'s `unique (asset_id, post_id, channel)` carries the comment "one file is used once per post per channel", and does not do that: Postgres treats NULLs as DISTINCT and `channel` is null for every ordinary attachment, so the constraint permits unlimited duplicates for precisely the shape that always occurs. A partial unique index over the null-channel rows closes it. **The exhaustive-status sweep that proved nothing.** `for (const s of PostStatusSchema.options) { const locks = LOCKING_POST_STATUSES.includes(s); expect(decide([site(s)]).ok).toBe(!locks) }` reads the module's own constant to compute what it expects, so it is self-consistent under any mutation of that constant — dropping `partial` flipped the expectation and the result together and 32 of 33 tests stayed green. Rewritten as a hand-written `Record<PostStatus, boolean>` plus a completeness assertion against the live enum, the same mutation is caught by three. Mutation-tested in both languages: 3 SQL mutants and 2 TS mutants, each shown red and restored green.
2026-08-20 · apps/web (wt-editor2) — **`turbo build` and `next dev` share `apps/web/.next`, and a dev server started on top of a production build fails 33 of 63 smoke tests with an error that names neither.** Running the gate legs in the order `pnpm gate` states them — smoke, then build — is fine. Running BUILD and then smoke again is not: the production build leaves `.next` in a state the dev server reuses, and every page then answers `Could not find the module "[project]/apps/web/src/app/(app)/error.tsx#default" in the React Client Manifest`, which reads like an application bug and is an artefact-directory collision. The failures are FAST (1.5–1.8s, not timeouts) and they hit specs that have nothing to do with the change under test — `no-impossible-remedy`, `no-truncated-labels`, `topbar` — which is the tell: a change that breaks unrelated screens instantly did not break them, the server did. `journalctl -k` showed no OOM and memory was fine, so the usual suspect was ruled out first. Deleting `apps/web/.next` and re-running took 63/63 with zero flaky. **Delete `.next` between a build and any dev-server run**, and treat a burst of sub-2s failures across unrelated specs as an environment verdict rather than a code one.

2026-08-20 · packages/publishing + apps/web (wt-editor2) — **A limit that lives on a channel may be a FORMAT's limit wearing the channel's clothes, and stacking the two refuses the exact input the format requires.** `CONSTRAINTS.instagram.imageDims.aspectRange` is `[0.8, 1.91]` and has read as a channel rule since the Constraint Engine was written. It is not one: it is the FEED format's rule, sitting on the channel because `PlatformSpec` is frozen and has nowhere else to put it. An Instagram Story is 9:16, i.e. **0.56** — so the moment a format check was added ALONGSIDE the engine's, a Story became unattachable while every message on screen told the writer their upright photo was the wrong shape for an upright format. The fix is that a format declaring its own aspect rule REPLACES the engine's for that attachment (`decideAttach` filters `MEDIA_ASPECT`, and only that code — a mutant dropping every engine verdict is killed by a 100×100 story still failing `MEDIA_DIMS`). The general shape: before adding a dimension to a frozen contract's neighbours, ask which of the contract's existing fields were ALREADY that dimension, unnamed. Three more findings from the same lane, each from a different instrument. **Reading every user-facing sentence, not every assertion**, caught two messages that would have shipped a raw lowercase enum: `presentViolation` rewrites the channel key ONLY when it LEADS the message, so "Sahoda can't publish video to gbp yet." and "A instagram story…" rendered exactly like that — and the same read found that `violation-copy`'s allowlist had never listed `MEDIA_REQUIRED` or `MEDIA_ASPECT`, so Instagram's two most important sentences (the two doc 13 §10 said would go live the instant Instagram became publishable) both degraded to "This does not meet the channel rules." **Reading a screenshot, not a DOM assertion**, caught the "cannot suggest hashtags yet" note printing once per version card — four identical apologies on one screen — and two red lines about one missing photo. **Mutating by line rather than by string** caught a FALSE SURVIVOR: the patch matched text prettier had since wrapped across three lines, so the mutation silently no-op'd and looked exactly like a guard that does not work. And a racy assertion of my own — waiting for "Save all versions" to disappear, which is satisfied the instant the label reads "Save this version" — nearly had me file the app's correct behaviour as a data-loss bug. Also recorded: `platforms[].platformSpecificData` on Zernio's publish call had no caller, which made every channel-specific control UNREACHABLE rather than unused (it has no root-level equivalent), and the GBP call-to-action was collected into `extras.gbpCta` and read by nothing — a control that does nothing, which is worse than an absent one because an absent control makes no promise.
2026-08-20 · apps/jobs + apps/web + packages/shared + scripts (wt-collect) — **The nightly metrics job has never collected once, and it needed no merge and no deploy to start — but arming it first required un-learning three things about this environment, and one of them was a defect I had just introduced.** The cause was already measured by run 30: production builds from `wt-web`, which contains neither `/api/cron/metrics` nor its `vercel.json` entry, and Vercel cannot schedule a path that is not in the build. `runMetricCapture` imports neither the Trigger.dev SDK nor anything from Next, so a scheduled GitHub Action runs it directly. **THE READ-ONLY RUNNER THIS REPO KEEPS RECOMMENDING IS A WRITE HAZARD THROUGH A POOLER.** The pattern recorded from earlier lanes opens a connection and issues `set default_transaction_read_only = on`. Through supavisor's TRANSACTION pooler that SET persists on the shared backend and is handed to whoever gets it next: the very next process — the capture — died with `cannot execute INSERT in a read-only transaction` on a connection that had never asked for one, and ten probe connections all landed on the same backend pid still carrying it. In production that backend is the app. `begin read only` scopes the same protection to the transaction and cannot leak. **THE DIRECT DATABASE HOST IS UNREACHABLE FROM CI FOR THE SAME REASON IT WAS UNREACHABLE FROM VERCEL:** `db.<ref>.supabase.co` publishes 1 AAAA record and 0 A records, and a GitHub-hosted runner has no IPv6 route. The working pooler is `aws-1-ap-south-1`, not `aws-0-` — `aws-0` resolves, accepts the connection and answers `Tenant or user not found`, which reads like a credential problem and is a region problem. The runner prints the host CLASSIFICATION, never the value, so that is a first-line diagnosis instead of a five-minute hang. **AND `supabase db push` COULD NOT BE USED TO APPLY THE ONE MIGRATION THAT NEEDED APPLYING:** production's `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` records 36 versions against 43 files on this branch, and SEVEN of the nine unrecorded ones are already applied in substance — `post_metric_snapshots`, `templates`, `assets`, `campaigns`, `campaign_posts`, `post_variants.version` and `.format` all exist. A push would have re-run them. One file, in one transaction, behind a guard shown refusing `drop`/`truncate`/unqualified `delete`/unqualified `update` before it opens a connection at all. PostgREST then still answered `PGRST205` until `notify pgrst, 'reload schema'`; the schema cache is a separate layer from the database and applying a migration does not touch it. **COLLECTION IS REAL NOW:** 7 targets, 6 measured, 1 unreadable, 18 numbers, 6 new rows, and — the part that is the whole point — SEVEN (post, channel, day) combinations carry NO ROW rather than a zero, including a LinkedIn post unreadable on two consecutive days. Three further runs wrote 0 rows each, because the conflict key is the DAY. **A PROGRAM CANNOT MAKE A PIPE RETURN ITS OWN EXIT STATUS.** `pnpm gate | tail -60` reports `tail`'s status, `fish` has no `pipefail`, and no amount of cleverness inside the gate changes the shell's rule — so the fix is to remove the REASON to pipe (per-stage logs on disk, one summary line each, the failing stage's tail printed automatically) and to make the verdict survive a pipe anyway: shouted on STDERR, which `| tail` does not redirect; the last line of stdout, so it survives `tail -n` for any n; and `.gate/verdict.json`, so `pnpm gate | tail -60 ; pnpm gate:verdict` turns it back into an exit code. `gate:verdict` REFUSES on a missing file, because 'no gate has run' and 'the gate passed' are the same mistake in a new place. Demonstrated red through the pipe and green after. **AN INSTRUMENT THAT REPORTS AND ONE THAT ENFORCES, AGAIN — AND ARMING IT FOUND THREE LIVE DEFECTS IN THIRTY SECONDS.** `shell-probe.spec.ts` measured the 44px touch floor, asserted nothing, was skipped unless `DESIGN_AUDIT=1` and carried no `@smoke` tag. Made to assert, it went red immediately: the phone brand mark rendered **26x44** — `95ed24f`'s commit message logs it as 'Sahoda mark 26 -> 44' and only `min-h` was ever added, so the hit area was 18px short ACROSS for three days behind a probe that printed the number; the collapsed workspace switcher was 40 wide, 4px short, left there by `2f9fca1`'s own fix; and `CreateWorkspaceButton` was **164x34**, because it is a bespoke `<button>` with its own class string and the kit's `max-narrow:min-h-[44px]` in `ui/button.tsx` went round it — the third time that same file has been the sibling a shell-wide fix missed. Desktop is asserted to STAY dense (>0 controls under 44 at 1440), so a future floor fix cannot leak upward, which is exactly the regression `2f9fca1` had to undo. Two selector lessons: `header a` matches every CARD header too and pulled three 'View all' links into a shell measurement, and `[class*=bottom]` matched a `bottom-0` utility on an in-page empty state — a guard that names the wrong element is worse than one that names nothing, because somebody will go and fix it. **`perDayCap` WAS DECLARED ON ALL FOUR CHANNELS AND READ BY NOTHING** — four declarations, zero call sites, since the Constraint Engine's first commit. An unenforced limit still LOOKS like a limit to everyone reading the specs. Wired on `runPublishPost`, before the X ration (the platform's rule is universal; the ration is Sahoda's money and applies to one channel), with the same three-way discipline: an unreadable count is neither 'exhausted' nor '0 used'. Five mutants killed. Two of the ration's own tests had to be REWRITTEN rather than kept — 'LinkedIn is never counted' stopped being true the moment LinkedIn got a cap of its own, and the guarantee that survives is 'LinkedIn is never asked about a MONTH window'. **AND X'S DECLARED 100/DAY IS UNREACHABLE BY CONSTRUCTION**, because the monthly ration is lower than one day's platform cap; recorded in a test so that raising the ration past 100 cannot silently make the daily limit load-bearing. **THE X RATION WAS A GUESS AND THE ARITHMETIC IS BRUTAL.** X has had no free tier since 2026-02-06; its own developers' account states $0.015 a post and **$0.20 with a URL** — 13.3x — and an SMB marketing post carries a link. 40 posts is $8.00 a workspace a month, which is **67% of Starter's entire $12** on one channel, before the aggregator fee that already dominates marginal cost. Lowered to 12 ($2.40 worst case; 20% of Starter, 8% of Growth, 3% of Agency) with the full derivation in the constant. The honest caveat is in there too: one global constant has to be safe for the weakest plan that can reach X, and on Free any number above zero is a loss — the right shape is a per-plan ration on `PlanLimits`, which is a frozen-contract change and was not made here.

2026-08-20 · billing / the plan lifecycle (wt-money) — **A compensating entry larger than the balance does not clamp itself. It writes NOTHING, and the chargeback becomes invisible.** Measured against the real `app.apply_ledger_entry` under PGlite: `GRANT 1500`, `DEBIT 1300`, balance 200, then `ADJUST -1500` → `violates check constraint "balance_held_le_total"`, transaction aborted, balance still 200, ledger still two rows. Not `balance_total_nonneg`, which is the one you would predict — with `held = 0` the held-vs-total check trips first, so a guard matching only the expected constraint name would have missed it. That is strictly worse than an imperfect record: the money has left the bank account either way and now nothing knows. So `applyReversal` clamps to AVAILABLE (`total - held`, because held credits bound a reversal as firmly as the total does — measured to the exact unit: `-201` fails and `-200` succeeds on a 1000/800 balance) and reports the remainder as `shortfallCredits`, which is deliberately NOT forced into the ledger. Credits are a delivered good and consumed credits cannot be un-consumed; what is outstanding is MONEY, and it belongs on the credit note as a receivable rather than as a negative balance the schema forbids and which would misdescribe the debt anyway. `reversed + shortfall === requested` is a zod refinement, not a convention. **THE SAME MEASUREMENT FORCES THE PLAN-CHANGE ASYMMETRY**: an upgrade is immediate, a downgrade ALWAYS waits for the period boundary, because credits already granted may already be spent and there is no version of an immediate downgrade that leaves the ledger both honest and complete. The clamp is also retried, because reading the balance and then applying the ADJUST is check-then-act — a concurrent DEBIT makes even the clamped amount too large — and retrying under the SAME idempotency key is safe precisely because the failed attempt wrote nothing. **THE ESCALATION GUARD, and the obvious move that is one:** a pending downgrade needs a write to `subscriptions`, and apps/web has no service-role client by design. Giving the table a tenant UPDATE policy — the obvious answer — lets any member run `update subscriptions set plan_id = 'agency'` and hand themselves Agency entitlements for nothing. `public.set_pending_plan_change` is SECURITY DEFINER, takes identity from `auth.jwt()`, is owner-only, and REFUSES anything not strictly cheaper; a "pending upgrade" applied by the period-end sweeper would be a free one. Shown refusing in the suite, not merely present. **A MONEY BUG THAT LOOKS LIKE IDEMPOTENCY WORKING:** `monthlyGrantKey` is (plan, period, workspace) with no change in it, so a mid-period upgrade to a plan already granted this month REPLAYS — `replayed: true`, zero credits, and the webhook route reporting success for a payment that took real money. Two upgrades in one month is not exotic, it is what a growing customer does. Keyed on `planChangeGrantKey(changeId)` instead, with the prorated amount and credits carried in `order_tags` (the only carrier a Cashfree webhook has) and the amount guard switched to reconcile against the TAGGED figure — bounded by the plan's own monthly allotment, because "an attacker cannot set this" and "our own proration cannot compute this wrongly" are different claims and only the first is true. **POSTGREST DOES NOT REPORT A MISSING TABLE AS 42P01.** Measured against the live project: missing TABLE → HTTP 404 `PGRST205` "Could not find the table 'public.invoices' in the schema cache" (resolved from PostgREST's own cache, Postgres never sees it); missing COLUMN → HTTP 400 `42703`. This mattered because the migration is applied by hand and had not been, so the first browser render of /settings/plan said **"Sahoda could not read your plan just now"** on a perfectly healthy account — the exact false-diagnosis-plus-impossible-remedy shape `no-impossible-remedy.spec.ts` exists to catch, written by me, in the file that knows about it. The repair is a FOURTH read state (`unavailable`) and a split select, so the plan's base columns are read on their own and the lifecycle columns are simply absent until the migration lands. That also means the migration can be applied on its own schedule instead of needing a lockstep deploy. **THREE UI TRAPS, all invisible to typecheck and all caught by looking at the rendered page.** (1) `globals.css:45` sets `--breakpoint-*: initial`, which DELETES Tailwind's default breakpoints — only `narrow` (700px) and `wide` (1180px) exist. `sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-4` compiles, ships, and does nothing; four tiles rendered full-width stacked at 1280px. There is no warning for this, only the screenshot. (2) Putting `.is-real` on the current-plan card reproduced docs/27 §3.2 verbatim — a ~1,000px solid ORANGE block, the loudest object in the product, on the money screen. A rung is a CHIP's signature; §1.1 allows a brand fill on "a button, chip, badge, active nav wash" and a card is none of those. The deeper rule: certainty marking only informs where certainty VARIES, and nobody is asking whether the plan they are on is real. The rungs earn their place on the scheduled change (`.is-committed`) and the priced-but-unagreed preview (`.is-proposed`), where pressing the button is literally the act that moves a claim up a rung. (3) `type-h1` on a `<p>` gave the whole screen no heading outline at all; the browser check found it because `getByRole('heading')` matched nothing. **A GUARD THAT ARGUED AGAINST ITS OWN GUARANTEE:** the dunning copy test forbade `/deleted|removed|lost/i` to stop a claim that a customer's work was destroyed — and it fired on "Nothing has been deleted", which is the reassurance the whole feature exists to make. The forbidden thing is the affirmative claim, not the word. **TWO TOOLING FACTS.** `test.poolOptions` was REMOVED in Vitest 4, so a `maxThreads` cap written in the Vitest 3 spelling is silently ignored — and the suite then passed anyway on a quiet moment, which would have been read as proof the cap worked. The only tell is a `DEPRECATED` line in the output. And PGlite suites are CPU-bound, not I/O-bound, which breaks the assumption file parallelism rests on: a fifth `.pglite.` file tipped four unrelated ones into `Hook timed out` in `beforeAll` while merely booting their database. `fileParallelism: false` in packages/db makes it deterministic for about a minute of wall clock. On a machine running three sibling sessions, `turbo run test` also needed `--concurrency=1` to be reproducible; every failure without it was a timeout that passed standalone. **BLOCKED, with evidence:** this deployment's `CASHFREE_APP_ID` and `CASHFREE_SECRET_KEY` hold the identical 40-character value, and a real sandbox Create Order returns `HTTP 401 {"message":"authentication Failed","code":"request_failed","type":"authentication_error"}`. No order can be opened from here at all, so the `/billing/checkout/{orderId}` bridge route — which had never existed, leaving `CheckoutSession.url` pointing at a 404 one env flip from a paying customer — ships reading the real order and stating plainly that the payment step is not connected, rather than shipping an SDK integration that could not be exercised even once. **AND ONE DEFECT THIS LANE DID NOT TOUCH:** the rail foot still renders `100 of —`, the exact `100 of —` docs/27 §3 names as the clearest instance of the pattern the absence vocabulary exists to end. It is in the shell, not this lane's files, and is reported rather than fixed to avoid a silent conflict with the design lane.
2026-08-19 · apps/web + apps/jobs + packages/publishing (wt-conn) — **A column named `published` is not evidence of a publish, and the cheapest cap to build is the one counting the wrong rows.** /connections was asked for an X post cap: show remaining, refuse before spending. The obvious counter is `post_variants where channel='x' and publish_status='published'`, and MEASURED against production it returns 3 — every one a fixture whose permalink begins `fixture://`. Nothing reached X and nothing was billed, so that meter would have shown a customer spend that never happened and eventually refused them over money nobody paid. `post_publish_logs` carries `mode`, and filtered to `mode='live' and status='succeeded'` the same data returns **0 live X sends, ever**. That one query also settles the readiness marks the screen renders — instagram 6 live successes, linkedin 1, x and gbp 0 — so PUBLISHES TODAY and BUILT NOT PROVEN are evidence rather than intent, and re-running it is how they are re-earned. The brief's own premise was stale in a way worth recording: it asked to surface X's tiny free tier, and X has had no free tier since February 2026 — the API is pay-per-use at $0.015 a post and **$0.200 when the post carries a link**, 13.3×, which is very nearly every marketing post an SMB writes. There was no X-imposed monthly allowance left to render “247 of 500” against, and rendering one anyway would have been the `100 of —` failure docs/26 §4 names: a numerator, the word “of”, and a denominator that does not exist. So the denominator is Sahoda's, said in words on the line under the number, and only the numerator is measured. Two more things fell out. `PlatformSpec.perDayCap` has sat on all four channel specs since the Constraint Engine landed and is read by NOTHING — a cap that lives in the type system and has never once run. And the seven Postgres CHECK constraints mirroring `ChannelSchema` (connections, post_variants, post_publish_logs, inbox_threads, post_metric_snapshots, asset_usages, templates) turned out to be the feature rather than the obstacle when adding Facebook, YouTube, Pinterest and Telegram: a planned channel has no adapter, so it must never look connectable, and a type that CANNOT be written to `connections.platform` is the strongest guarantee of that available. Finally, on verification — the e2e harness looked broken here and was merely starved: `/connections` took **57.8 seconds** to compile on a dev server contending with four other worktrees, which ate the 30s sign-in budget and failed the pre-existing `unauthenticated` spec too. Against `next start` on a production build the same route served in **18ms** and the suite passed. A slow dev server and a broken test read identically in the output; timing the route is what tells them apart.

2026-08-13 · apps/web (wt-web) — **A reported regression can be real as an observation and false as a cause; one query settles it faster than any amount of reading.** The report: two August workspaces have a brand and no theme, every July one has both, so the rebuilt onboarding must have stopped calling `saveWorkspaceTheme`. It calls it (`onboarding-flow.tsx` `handleFinish`), guarded by `colors.length > 0`. The tempting fix — write a default-palette row when nothing was extracted — would have been ACTIVELY harmful three ways: `workspace_themes.source` is `'extracted'`, so the row fabricates provenance; `brandSkinVars` already falls back to `DEFAULT_PRIMARY`, so it paints the identical orange and changes nothing anyone sees; and `theme.test.ts` pins `saveWorkspaceTheme([])` → `ok:false`, correctly. What settled it was the database: of 18 workspaces, 9 have a brain, and brain-without-theme is 3 of 8 in JULY — including 67TEAM with 16 brains and 0 themes on 20 Jul — against exactly ONE August workspace with a brain at all. "Every July one has both" is false by three counterexamples, and a single August row cannot establish a trend. The mechanism the observation was circling is real but different: July's palette came from an uploaded logo through canvas extraction, and the rebuilt door reads colours DECLARED by a fetched page, so a sentence door yields `colors: []` by construction — which the step already says on screen ("we did not find a colour here"). Disclosed behaviour, not silent breakage. **THE DEFECT THE HUNT ACTUALLY FOUND HAS NO SYMPTOM AT ALL.** The version lookup in `saveWorkspaceTheme` carried no `workspace_id` filter, so it read the max across every workspace RLS lets the caller see. It never broke: UNIQUE is (workspace_id, version) and a global max is always ≥ the local one, so the value stayed unique and every insert succeeded. Nothing in the result, the row, or the database would ever have shown it. When the only observable is the QUERY, the query is what the test must assert — the mock now records `.eq()` filters, and the mutation (delete the `.eq`) kills that test and nothing else. **SECOND FINDING, same shape: a static check that cannot see the code it checks reports the opposite of the truth.** `PENDING_ANCHORS['connections.connect_x']` said connecting was BLOCKED by a missing INSERT policy. `pg_policies` confirms `connections` still has only select/update/delete — and connecting works anyway, on all four platforms, because the write goes through `upsert_zernio_connection`, which is SECURITY DEFINER. The literal fact was true and load-bearing for a conclusion that was false. The entry survived the integrity test's own anti-staleness case ("nothing is listed as pending while it is actually rendered") because that case scans for `data-guide="…"` LITERALS and the control composes its anchor inside a `.map()`, so no literal can ever appear. A registry of exemptions needs its exemptions to expire: the claim moved to `DYNAMIC_ANCHORS`, which asserts the opposite thing, is checked against the named component still carrying a `data-guide`, and is backed by a render test reading the composed value off the DOM. **THIRD, on the Alpha record itself:** it listed failing codes, which quietly made the other eight items read as passes. Adding `partial` to a failure LIST makes that worse — with fails and partials both named, the unnamed remainder looks doubly confirmed. Enumerate all fourteen with a status and evidence each; and do NOT restamp `recordedOn` when only six were re-read, because a fresh date claims a fresh look at eight nobody took. A8 was never a pass, only absent from a list: every x and gbp row in `post_publish_logs` is `mode:'fixture'`, and the 7 live successes belong to instagram and linkedin, which A8 does not name. **AND A PROCESS NOTE:** rewriting this file programmatically to move its heading silently ate a space after a backtick in two unrelated 19 Jul entries. `git diff --numstat` looked fine and the entry COUNT was right; only diffing the entry openers against HEAD caught it. Reorder lines, never reflow prose you did not write.

2026-08-13 · apps/web (wt-status) — **A convention that has already failed twice will fail a third time; the third fix has to be a type.** `posts.status` is stale by design — the publish path writes `post_variants` and never touches the post row — and three separate consumers read it as an outcome: `autoPublishTruth`, which told four production posts "nothing was published" while their own chips two inches above said published; `LiveStatusBadge`, which gated `.is-real` on `status === 'published'` and so drew a post live on every channel as merely `committed`; and the week grid + home week strip, where the strip had no variant data at all and could never render a real publish.
