Each was written by someone with no reason to know, because `post.status` is the most natural thing to type and it compiled. So it no longer compiles: `DisplayPost` is `Omit<Post,'status'>` plus an `intent` field and a `status` sealed behind a `unique symbol`, and only `forDisplay` at the page boundary converts. The obvious version, `status?: never`, is NOT enough and this is the trap worth carrying — it types the field as `undefined`, and TypeScript permits `undefined === 'published'`, so the guard compiles a comparison that is silently always false and renders as "not published yet" on a post that is live. A type with no overlap with `string` makes it TS2367. Three more things fell out. Splitting the certainty signature was not enough on its own: the chip's WORD still said "Approved" over a solid fill, so `displayStatus` had to move too. `post_publish_logs.mode` turned out to be a second, independent derivation of "simulated" off a different table with its own failure mode, so `listPublishModes` is deleted rather than kept in parallel — one fact, one derivation. And zero variant rows must mean `unknown`, never `none`: `listVariantStates` returns an empty map on ANY read failure, so collapsing the two would let one outage demote every published post on the page. Proven by mutation: 11 behavioural mutants killed (4 of them by folding `simulated` into `live`), and 6 type mutants held in `apps/web/type-mutants/`, compiled deliberately by `display-post.guard.test.ts` because `turbo typecheck` proves the opposite property — that the code which exists compiles — and would stay green if the guard were quietly removed.

2026-08-12 · onboarding / the two-stack merge (wt-web) — **Swapping a "worse" implementation for a "better" package was very nearly a security regression, and only reading both comments caught it.** wt-brain's `packages/research` and wt-onboard's `fetch-site.ts` both fetch a founder-supplied URL from our own server — the classic SSRF shape. The instruction was to keep research's SSRF guard and delete the other. But research's own header said, honestly, **"KNOWN RESIDUAL: DNS rebinding. We resolve, check, then fetch, and a hostile resolver can answer differently between those two steps"** — while the file being deleted had already closed exactly that, because `node:http` accepts a `lookup` function that the SOCKET calls at connect time, so there is no second resolution left to disagree with. Node's global `fetch` has no such hook; undici's dispatcher does, but undici is not a dependency and adding one is a stop-and-ask, so the lower-level client is the dependency-free answer. Ported as `pinned-fetch.ts` and wired in under the EXISTING `fetchImpl` seam — that is what made it a small change instead of a rewrite: `safeFetch`'s redirect loop, byte cap and content-type rules never moved. **THE BLIND SPOT CAME WITH IT:** Node does not resolve a host that is already an IP literal, so `http://127.0.0.1/` and `http://169.254.169.254/` never reach `lookup` at all — the original found this by executing it against a live loopback server, and the ported test keeps a `served` counter for the same reason, because "the connection was never made" is a property no mock can demonstrate. A second test fetches a real public URL from a `.live.test.ts`, because every other assertion is that a request was REFUSED and a transport that refused everything would pass all of them while shipping a crawler that reads nothing. LESSON: when two lanes solve the same problem, the one you are keeping is not automatically stronger on every axis — diff the THREAT MODELS, not the file counts. Also kept from the loser: the reserved-range list (`192.0.0.0/24`, TEST-NET-1/2/3, `198.18.0.0/15`), which research omitted.

2026-08-12 · onboarding / canvas-bound extraction (wt-web) — **A function's TYPE can tell you it cannot run where you need it, and the workaround is usually a better signal anyway.** `extractPalette(img: HTMLImageElement)` reads pixels through a canvas, so a Brand Skin derived from a logo works only in the browser — there is no way to run it on a page fetched server-side, and decoding the logo in Node means a new image dependency for one meta tag's worth of colour. wt-onboard's answer was to stop looking at what the page LOOKS like and read what it DECLARES: `<meta name="theme-color">` outranks everything because it is a deliberate statement, otherwise the most-repeated non-neutral `#hex`/`rgb()` in the markup, with near-white, near-black and near-grey rejected as the page furniture every site on earth has. That is a CHOSEN colour rather than an average of pixels, and it is free because the markup is already in hand. **THE PLUMBING PROBLEM IT CREATED, and the shape of the fix:** `@sahoda/research` converts HTML to markdown and throws the markup away — every consumer wants prose — so there was nothing left to read a meta tag out of. Retaining `html` on `ScrapedPage` would have held five pages of markup to serve one of them. A callback on the direct source, invoked from `map()` only, exposes exactly one page's HTML **by construction rather than by discipline**, and tiers 2 and 3 (which return text and markdown) simply never call it, so a site read by either yields no colours — already a first-class answer. SECOND FINDING, from the same merge: **a flow replaced wholesale silently un-does work done on the file it replaced.** Taking wt-onboard's `onboarding-flow.tsx` whole brought back a `saveBrandMemory(brain, source)` call written before `confirmPaths` existed, so every card a user corrected in the reveal would have saved unconfirmed and /brain would have opened at "0 of 15" after a full onboarding — the exact misreading the ring exists to prevent. `git checkout --theirs` resolves the CONFLICT and tells you nothing about the INTEGRATION.

2026-08-12 · Brand Brain / provenance reconciliation (wt-web) — **Two lanes shipped two answers to “who wrote this field”, and the one that needed no migration was the one that made the best interaction impossible.** wt-brainui derived provenance by DIFFING the append-only version history: for each field find the version where its value last changed, read that version's `source`. Elegant, no schema change, and two correct behaviours fell out for free — a regenerate returning the SAME text left a confirmation standing, a regenerate returning DIFFERENT text reverted the field to a guess. Its cost was invisible until you name it: **a user who reads a guess, agrees with it, and wants to say so produces an IDENTICAL payload, and an identical payload records no change.** Confirming a guess was unrepresentable; the editor refused the write and told them to “change something to confirm this field”. wt-brain's `FieldMetaSchema` (`confirmed` + `source`) is authoritative instead. **THE BLOCKER THAT WASN'T:** FieldMeta is a v2 contract, v2 is referenced only by its own test and an unrun migration script, and v2's meta is per-SECTION while the ring counts per-LEAF — so a literal swap reads `undefined` on every real row and the ring reads 0 forever. Adopted at v1 leaf granularity instead, as a `field_meta` key ON the payload. **No migration was needed and the reason is worth keeping:** `resolve_brand_memory` validates that the six sections are objects and pins three array lengths — it does not reject ADDITIONAL keys, and 32 KB of ceiling sits over a ~2 KB brain. And it cannot bloat the model prompt, because `brand-context.ts` re-parses the stored row with the UNEXTENDED `BrandMemoryPayloadSchema` before prepending it to every call, and zod strips unknown keys — the protection is a default nobody has to remember. `brand_guidelines` still declares the unextended schema as its output, so the model has no channel to write provenance at all, which is the same property `ExtractedFieldSchema` buys with `confirmed: z.literal(false)`. **THE DEFECT I WROTE AND CAUGHT:** my first draft treated “changed while saving as `manual`” as authorship. True for a single-field edit; badly wrong for onboarding, where a fresh resolve differs from the previous brain in nearly every field and, with NO previous brain, in all fifteen — pressing Finish would have marked the whole brain confirmed. Confirmation is now never inferred from a diff: callers NAME the paths a person wrote, and `confirmPaths` is the complete statement of what a write claims. The two rules survive as the only other branch, stated explicitly, because reading a stored flag does not preserve them by accident. **THREE THINGS FELL OUT:** Finish writes ONE version instead of two (the model/user pair existed only to make the diff work); `historyComplete` and its whole degradation path are gone (a brain with no `field_meta` is not a degraded read, it is a brain nobody confirmed); and three tests that PINNED the old limitation as correct (“an unchanged value writes nothing”) had to be rewritten rather than satisfied — the same shape as the earlier “test suite proves the wrong thing” finding. **UNRESOLVED, reported not taken:** wt-brain's v2 taxonomy classes `brand_persona.*` and `hook.primary_emotion`/`sample_hooks` as `derived` — unconfirmable by definition — while /brain offers editors for all five and counts them; honouring it drops the ring's denominator 15 → 10. **DOC DRIFT SPOTTED:** the entry directly below this one and `url-door.ts`'s own docstring both say `applyExtractedFields` is “NOT wired into the resolve path”; wt-brain's final commit (56d4996) wired both doors, and the earlier prose was never updated. The behaviour is defensible — fields ride out separately as `DoorProvenance` with `confirmed:false` — but two files still assert the opposite.

2026-08-12 · repo / .gitignore (wt-web) — **`git check-ignore` lies about the files you most need it to tell the truth about.** It suppresses output for TRACKED paths, so a tracked file that every rule ignores reports clean — which is exactly how `apps/web/REQUESTS.md` sat matched by `*.md` (line 51) with no negation reaching it, surviving only because .gitignore does not apply to already-tracked files. Untrack it once and it vanishes from every fresh worktree, the same mechanism that dropped CLAUDE.md and LEARNINGS.md in the 2026-08-08 squash and the same shape as `.env.example` shadowed by `.env*`. **`--no-index` is the only honest audit**, and the loop worth keeping is `git ls-files '*.md' | while read f; do git check-ignore -q --no-index "$f" && echo "$f"; done` — it names every tracked file living on borrowed time. One hit across 102 tracked markdown files. **SECOND TRAP, hit live while verifying the fix:** with `-v`, check-ignore exits 0 when ANY pattern matches, INCLUDING a negation — so `check-ignore -v path && echo IGNORED` reports every protected file as ignored. `-q` is the truth test. My first verification pass printed six false alarms before the exit code was read properly. Fix went NARROW (`!/apps/web/REQUESTS.md`, one file) rather than `!/apps/*/REQUESTS.md`: two sibling REQUESTS.md files exist on disk and have never been tracked, so a class rule would un-ignore two files and the next `git add -A` would commit them unreviewed.

2026-08-12 · Brand Brain / the repair retry (wt-brain) — **A 2x cost ran unrecorded for weeks because `status: 'ok'` was true and useless.** `brand_extract` failed its schema on the FIRST attempt in 5 of 6 calls; the engine's one repair retry rescued every one, and a repaired call logged exactly what a clean call logged. The suspects were the strict schema features — `confirmed: z.literal(false)`, the `channel` enum, required `source_url`. **All three were wrong.** Instrumenting the first attempt (new `MeshRunnerDeps.onRepair`, carrying zod's own message and the raw text) showed the reason was `response was not valid JSON` every single time, and every sample ended MID-TOKEN: `"source_url": "https://fbs.edu.in/fbs-`, `problem-solving.`, `"confirmed": false,`. **The model was being cut off**: `MAX_TOKENS = 2048` against an answer that needs ~3,400. A too-small budget was buying a whole second call, and the second call resends the entire corpus. Fixed to 4096 → **repair rate 83% → 0%, cost per extraction $0.122 → $0.067 (45% cheaper)**, and input tokens went identical across all six trials, which is itself the proof no repair ran. LESSON: `JSON.parse` failing and zod failing are different diagnoses, and only one of them is about the schema — `safeParseOutput` collapsed both into one unsurfaced string. WHAT NATIVE JSON-SCHEMA MODE WOULD HAVE DONE: nothing. Structured output constrains SHAPE; it cannot un-truncate a response that hit its token ceiling. SECOND FIX, from the same evidence: the wire contract now omits `confirmed` entirely (stamped server-side — the model has no channel to claim confirmation at all, strictly stronger than rejecting `true`) and cites a page INDEX instead of repeating a full `source_url` per field (resolved from OUR list, so "never invent a source" stops being a prompt rule the model can disobey; an out-of-range index is dropped). THIRD: `error_code = 'JSON_REPAIRED'` beside `status: 'ok'` — `status` carries a DB CHECK of ('ok','error','fallback') and widening it is a migration in wt-db's lane, but `error_code` is free text and null on every clean call, so `where error_code = 'JSON_REPAIRED'` is an exact repair rate with no migration. **wt-db REQUEST: widen the status CHECK to include 'repaired', or add `repaired boolean not null default false`.** FOURTH — a trap: `.gitignore:51` is `*.md` (documentation untracked 2026-08-05), so the `packages/mesh/REQUESTS.md` this request was first written into **would never have been committed**. Existing .md files survive only because .gitignore does not apply to already-tracked files — the same shape as `.env.example` being shadowed by `.env*`. A new .md in this repo is a file you wrote and nobody will ever read. FIFTH: the safe corpus stripper (URLs + markdown syntax, link TEXT kept) ships at 22% fewer input tokens; the aggressive variant buys 30% and was HELD, because printing what it drops shows it deletes the school's contact email, all four bolded value pillars ("Exceptional Academics", "Activity-Based Learning", "Holistic Development", "Personalized Guidance" — which the extractor reads as brand.values) and the job-listing detail, alongside the nav repeats it was aimed at.

2026-08-12 · Brand Brain / the URL door (wt-brain) — **A default is a fabricated signal wearing the clothes of a real one, and the model reads it.** `ResolveInputSchema.voice.formality/energy` were `.default(3)`; `brand_guidelines` JSON.stringify()s the whole input; so every resolve since M1 told the model a founder had chosen "3 and 3". It believed us — the 2026-08-12 arm-B note credited "mid-range formality/energy scores" as intake it had received, and no screen has ever had a slider. Now `.optional()`, **not `.nullable()`**: the objective is an ABSENT KEY in the serialised prompt, because `null` is still a key the model reads. The tests assert on `JSON.stringify(input)`, not on the parsed object — that difference is between fixing this and moving it. Same family as the fabricated zero rejected in wt-analytics2: withholding an unanswered value costs nothing; supplying one invents evidence. **SECOND FINDING — a field mounted on one of two submit paths is mounted on neither.** Generate posts the `<form>` (carries anything with a `name=`); Regenerate never touches the form and builds FormData by hand. `description` had to be listed in both, and the guard is a test asserting the two SETS are equal rather than that either contains a key. **THE URL DOOR.** Firecrawl is a DIRECT integration in a new `packages/research`, not a mesh route — doc 18 opens by correcting exactly that conflation, and a scraper inside the Model Mesh makes a scrape failure look like a model failure. Chose `/v2/map` + N × `/v2/scrape` over async `/v2/crawl` + poll: bounded latency, no poll loop in a server action, and N is explicit so cost is knowable BEFORE spend. **Map bills 1 credit PER CALL** — the first counter tallied only scrapes and under-reported every signup by one; in a product whose rule is "costs shown before spend", an undercount is a promise. True cost is `pages + 1`. **QUARANTINE: the delimiters are the second line, not the first.** doc 18 §2 records twelve published prompt-injection defenses bypassed at >90%, so no framing is the guarantee. The guarantee is that `brand_extract` has no tools and its `confirmed` field is `z.literal(false)` — a page that wins the argument outright still cannot emit a confirmed field, reach a token, spend a credit or publish. Tested BOTH ways: the schema rejects `confirmed:true`, and a model that DOES obey the page fails the run rather than succeeding wrongly. **FAIL HONESTLY means distinguishing failures that look identical**: `unreachable` (nothing answered), `js_only` (answered 200, every page empty — the site is real and must never read as "your site is empty"), `thin` (real words, too few), and `crawler_error` (OUR bad key or exhausted credits, never reported as a fact about their website). No fallback payload anywhere on this path — inventing a voice and presenting it as extracted is the one thing §5 forbids outright. **NOT WIRED ON PURPOSE:** `applyExtractedFields` is not in the resolve path, because `ResolveInput` has no home for `confirmed` — extracted text landing in `source.one_liner` is indistinguishable from founder-typed prose, and a Brain resolved off it reads `moderate` with nobody having agreed. That is a fake-confirmation state, and the frozen contract cannot currently prevent it.

2026-08-12 · Brand Brain (wt-brain) — **Measured: the weak lock was the intake, but not the part of the intake we could fix by plumbing.** Every model-resolved Brand Brain in the live database reads `signal_lock: 'weak'` — 6 of 6; the only `strong` one has `source='manual'`, a human wrote it. Each weak note says the same sentence: "intake provided almost no signals beyond the name and category." They were right. `sparkToResolveInput` parsed ONLY `source.name` and `source.category` while the Spark screen went on asking for website and Instagram, and a test asserted that dropping them was deliberate. WHAT THE MEASUREMENT SHOWED (3 real resolves, one brand, one model, one day — `brand-intake.live.test.ts`, claude-sonnet-5, $0.103): **A** name+category → weak. **B** +website+instagram → **still weak**. **C** +description → **moderate**. Forwarding the two dropped fields bought NOTHING, because a URL is unfetchable and worth about its domain name; the lock moved only when real prose arrived. So the fix that looks like the fix (plumb the fields) is not the fix — doc 18 §5's URL door, where Firecrawl turns the URL into content, is load-bearing and not optional. The qualitative gap is wider than the lock: arms A/B produced red lines that fit any café ("no unverified health claims about tea"), arm C produced ones only this shop could have ("never imply the reading room or book club is pay-to-enter"), which is the half doc 18 §1 says earns the money. Arm C's own note then named the remaining gap — mission, customer pain/fear, archetype, hook lever still blank — so the model will tell you which channel to ask for next if you read the note. **REVERSES OWNER RULING #2** (2026-07-18, in this file): `caption_rewrite` was deliberately brand-less because "injecting brand voice would fight the user's text". The counter-argument, and the reason it was reversed on request: the directive already promised "clearer and more on-brand" while the task had no brand to be on. The original concern is real and now rests on the SYSTEM prompt ("preserve @mentions, #hashtags and links exactly") rather than on withholding the brain. SECOND-ORDER: `cachePrefix` and `buildMessages` are two independent halves and each fails silently alone — the def alone makes the engine fetch a brain that `buildMessages` then discards, and `expect(def.cachePrefix).toBe('brand_context')` (how content_variants and plan_week are tested) passes through that defect. Both mutations were executed against the new tests. THIRD: the documented live-smoke command never ran anything — `vitest.config.ts` excludes `*.live.test.ts`, and a filter matching nothing after the exclude prints "No test files found" and exits **0**. Another green that measured nothing; `vitest.live.config.ts` is the inverse config that actually runs them.

2026-08-09 · apps/web (wt-web) — **A diagnostic header is not a verdict, and the reported symptom was not the bug.** A manual call to /api/cron/sweeps 401'd carrying `x-clerk-auth-message: Invalid JWT form` and `x-clerk-auth-status: signed-out`, which reads as "Clerk blocked this". It did not. clerkMiddleware annotates EVERY response it touches, including ones it lets straight through; the message only means Clerk tried to read the `Bearer <CRON_SECRET>` as a JWT and could not. The route was already public (middleware.ts since the root commit) and already working — production logs showed 288 x 200 on the exact 5-minute cron cadence against 6 x 401, all of them off-cadence and all of them the manual attempts. WHAT SETTLED IT: the 401's own shape, not reasoning about headers. The route's 401 is `text/plain` with the body `Unauthorized`; Clerk's block on a genuinely protected route is a **404** of `text/html` whose `x-clerk-auth-reason` carries `protect-rewrite`. That token is the discriminator — present when Clerk intervenes, absent when it annotates — and `Invalid JWT form` appears in BOTH columns, which is exactly why it misled. THE REAL FINDING, which nobody asked for: probing the 401 with a JWT-SHAPED garbage token (`aaa.bbb.ccc` — three dot-separated parts that are not base64url) returned **500 MIDDLEWARE_INVOCATION_FAILED on every matched route including /sign-in and /embed/beta**. Clerk passes its own is-it-JWT-shaped check, then throws in the decode with nothing catching it. One header, no credentials, whole site. 2-part, 4-part and valid-base64url 3-part all 401 correctly, so only that one shape reaches it. FIXED IN TWO HALVES BECAUSE NEITHER COVERS THE OTHER: config.matcher now excludes the three self-authenticating routes (the throw happens while Clerk computes request state, BEFORE the callback, so an early return inside it is unreachable — and both matcher entries needed the exclusion, since the second matches all of /api on its own); and clerkMiddleware is wrapped so a throw degrades instead of 500ing, which is the only thing that covers /sign-in. SECOND-ORDER LESSONS, three: the first push failed the Vercel BUILD (`invalid-route-source`) because path-to-regexp accepts a raw regex only inside a group — a loud, pre-deploy failure, and the reason production served the old SHA throughout; a transitive "does this reach Clerk?" grep silently returned clean because it ran from the wrong cwd and `grep || echo clean` cannot tell a missing file from a clean one, so the closure was re-run with an existence check; and the 503 I added was correct for a bearer header and wrong for a cookie — `__session` is carried automatically, so a corrupted one wedges a real person out of every page, and that branch now clears the cookie and redirects.
2026-08-08 · RULE — a predicate that names a state the code itself writes must include that state, and only execution proves it does. `claimVariant`'s lease had read correctly to every reviewer since it shipped: `publish_claimed_at < now() - make_interval(secs => $4)` beside `publish_status in ('pending','scheduled','failed')`. But claiming SETS `publishing`, which that list excluded, so the lease could only ever be evaluated against rows nobody had claimed — dead code guarding the exact case it was written for. A publisher killed mid-flight stranded its variant in `publishing` forever, which is the hazard this file's own CLAUDE.md states one table up (rule 3, `posts.status`), reproduced one table down. WHAT FOUND IT: refusing to accept a statement-shape test. The live suites cannot run here (`forbidden-target.ts` bans the one Supabase project by ref), so the fallback was to assert the SQL string — which would have passed, because the string was right-looking the whole time it was wrong. PGlite (Postgres in WASM, in-process, a devDependency) executes the predicate instead, and the fix was one word. SECOND-ORDER LESSON: the same session's other defect was `catch {}` in `runReconcileSweep`, where 25 connections and 25 publishes could all throw and return the counters of an idle tick under HTTP 200. Both are the same shape — code that cannot report the failure it was written to handle — and neither was visible in review. 15 mutants across three specs (`mutations/publish-lease.mjs`, `dead-account.mjs`, `reconcile-failures.mjs`) now hold both.
2026-07-26 · RULE — a null PostgREST error on UPDATE does not mean the write happened; assert on the ROW. RLS denies an UPDATE by making the row invisible, not by raising: zero rows are affected and PostgREST returns `error: null`, so a denied write and a successful one are indistinguishable from the response alone. INSERT is the exception — its WITH CHECK fails loudly — which is why this hides so well behind an insert-only RLS test. Found against the `ops_*` tables (SELECT policies only, all writes through SECURITY DEFINER RPCs) where a test asserting `expect(error).not.toBeNull()` on a denied UPDATE failed; the grep it prompted found the same shape live in `saveWorkspaceTheme`, which archived the previous active theme, branched on `supersedeError` alone, and would then INSERT a second `status:'active'` row — precisely the "two active rows, reader picks one arbitrarily" state its own comment said the archive-first ordering existed to prevent. WHAT TO DO: chain `.select(...)` onto the update and check the returned rows, or re-read and assert the postcondition. `planner.ts` and `posts.ts` already did the former (`.select('*').maybeSingle()` then `if (error || !data)`) and were clean. SECOND-ORDER LESSON: the existing theme test asserted the update was CALLED with the right table, patch and filters, and stayed green through the whole bug — a call is not an outcome. The replacement asserts no active row survives, and was mutation-checked (fix removed → test fails) rather than trusted because it was green.
2026-07-18 · Day 0 — repo config laid down.
2026-07-18 · pricing.config.json — source doc §9 ships malformed JSON (`rollover_cap_x` was trapped inside the `currency_note` string, so the trailing `: 2` broke parsing). Fixed in-repo by closing the string after `agency 15000` and promoting `rollover_cap_x` to a real numeric key (2); all pricing numbers unchanged. TODO: fix doc §9 upstream too.
2026-07-18 · Phase A frozen — monorepo + packages/shared contracts + packages/db (27 tables, RLS, atomic apply_ledger_entry, seeds) applied to the fresh cloud project; 21 shared + 16 live ledger/RLS tests green. Toolchain is current-gen: Zod 4 (use `.prefault({})`, not `.default({})`, for pre-parse object defaults), TS 7, Vitest 4.
2026-07-18 · Gotchas — (1) a jsonb-expression uniqueness needs a `create unique index`, NOT an inline table `unique(...)` (syntax error 42601). (2) Supabase direct DB endpoint presents a private CA chain → `pg` needs the Supabase CA (`SUPABASE_DB_CA_CERT`) or relaxed chain-verification for that host in tests. (3) supabase-js throws `PGRST125` if `SUPABASE_URL` carries a path — normalize to `new URL(url).origin`.
2026-07-18 · packages/mesh (wt-mesh H4–6) — brand-context seam + content_variants/plan_week/site_generate + apps/jobs AI wiring. Decisions: (1) Brand grounding is a runner concern, not a per-task one: the engine fetches the active Brand Brain (service-role PostgREST) and injects a `cache:true` system prefix ONLY for tasks whose `MeshTaskDef.cachePrefix === 'brand_context'`; the built prefix is cached by `${workspace}:${version}` so a Brain version bump refreshes it. Best-effort — a brand-fetch hiccup proceeds brand-less (real output, less grounded), never fails the action. (2) `caption_rewrite` is intentionally BRAND-LESS (no cachePrefix): it edits an existing caption in place (preserve mentions/#/links), so injecting brand voice would fight the user's text; ruling #2. brand_guidelines is also brand-less (it PRODUCES the brain). (3) Task input schemas that aren't cross-worktree seams live locally in `packages/mesh` (frozen `@sahoda/shared` only holds the OUTPUT contracts); content_variants embeds per-channel limits read from the shared Constraint Engine `CONSTRAINTS`.
2026-07-18 · packages/mesh (wt-mesh H2–4) — foundation + brand_guidelines + caption_rewrite, tests-first. Decisions: (1) `ai_model_routes` table is NOT in the frozen schema, so routing lives as typed config in `packages/mesh` (routing.ts `TIER_ROUTES`/`TASK_TIER`); `MeshTaskDef.tier` is the source, wt-db stays untouched. (2) Providers + telemetry use native `fetch` (OpenAI-compatible `/chat/completions` + PostgREST) — ZERO new runtime deps, so `pnpm-lock.yaml` never changed across the whole track. (3) The 3 OpenRouter keys are cost-isolated by _workload class_ (RESEARCH/TEXT/IMAGE), selected by tier — not blind rotation. (4) Engine policy: provider fallback chain handles transport failures; JSON validity is `zod-parse + exactly one repair on the responding provider`; a double JSON failure serves the demo-fallback (brand_guidelines only, `MeshResult.fallback:true`) or a typed PROVIDER_ERROR — never a mock. (5) `ai_provider_logs` insert row is `Omit<AiProviderLog,'id'|'created_at'>` — structurally carries only metadata, so decrypted payloads can't leak; a telemetry write failure never breaks the model action. (6) mesh is a Node package → `packages/mesh/tsconfig.json` needs `types:["node"]` (resolved from the hoisted root `@types/node`; no devDep/lockfile change).
2026-07-18 · packages/mesh (wt-mesh fix-forward, post-merge review) — (1) `run()` now wraps `execute()` in an outer try/catch backstop: any UNEXPECTED non-ProviderCallError throw (a `buildMessages`/`planAttempts`/`price` bug, or a provider that skipped ProviderCallError normalization) still writes exactly one ai_provider_logs row (`status='error'`, `error_code='UNEXPECTED_ERROR'`) and returns a typed PROVIDER_ERROR — never a raw promise rejection; locks the frozen "typed error + a row on every path" guarantees (engine.test.ts pins it by scripting a plain `Error`). (2) content_variants/site_generate channel invariants are prompt-enforced only; consumers must filter to requested channels.
2026-07-18 · packages/db (wt-db) — `public.bootstrap_workspace` shipped (SECURITY DEFINER signup RPC: atomic ws+owner+profile+100cr grant, per-user advisory-lock double-submit guard, owner-only replay). Gotchas: (1) the supabase CLI link state (`packages/db/supabase/.temp`, main checkout) points at a FOREIGN project ("VRDHHI", 21 unrelated migrations) — never trust `db push` via the link; derive `--db-url` from `.env`'s `SUPABASE_DB_URL` (its `supabase_migrations` history matches our files exactly). (2) vitest executes `describe.skipIf(true)` bodies at collection — construct supabase clients lazily or env-less checkouts fail instead of skip. (3) Postgres `btrim(text)` strips only U+0020 — validate blank-ish input with `[\s ]` regexes. (4) assert anon denial by code `42501`, not "any error", or a lost EXECUTE revoke hides behind the in-function AUTH_REQUIRED guard.
2026-07-19 · packages/billing (wt-billing H7–11, first 2h) — withCredits spine + pg ledger port + PaymentProvider seam + monthly grant, tests-first (35 green: unit + real-DB). Decisions: (1) `withCredits` derives the HOLD **attempt server-side** from ledger state via `LedgerPort.latestHold`: an unsettled hold is RESUMED (crash recovery replays the same key), else advance past the last settled attempt so a retry-after-failure gets a fresh hold instead of colliding with `HOLD_ALREADY_SETTLED`. The 3-method `LedgerPort` (apply/latestHold/balance) lets the wrapper be unit-tested against an in-memory fake; billing is the **first production TS caller** of `app.apply_ledger_entry` — direct `pg` Pool, positional 12-arg call (order = `ApplyLedgerInputSchema`), because the `app` schema isn't PostgREST-exposed. (2) Owner rulings baked in: **#1** the `'fixture'`/`'cashfree'` provider-enum widening ships from wt-db as a controlled amendment → the `billing_webhook_events` insert is **DEFERRED, flagged, never faked**; replay-safety meanwhile rests on the ledger's `monthlyGrantKey` (proven: a duplicate webhook with a _different_ provider event id still replays, so no double-grant). **#2** the `PaymentProvider` seam is billing-internal now, promote to `@sahoda/shared` post-Alpha. **#3** DEBIT records `action_type`+`object_ref`, `model_tier`/`cogs` null for now (enrich via the mesh seam later). **#4** interface is **`PaymentProvider`** — docs' `BillingService` is the alias. **#5** INVARIANT — entitlements are a **separate gate helper called BEFORE `withCredits` at every AI entry point** (not inside the wrapper); the gate helper itself is hours 3–4, not this window. (3) Plan grants come from `PLAN_CATALOG.monthlyCredits`, NOT `pricing.config.json` (which is action costs only). (4) `pg.ts` is the first **production** direct-pg connection; TLS reuses the `db.ts` pattern (`SUPABASE_DB_CA_CERT` → full chain verify, else encrypted-but-unverified for the Supabase host only) — **H19 hardening: ship/require the Supabase CA cert in prod** so `rejectUnauthorized:true`. (5) `plans.stripe_price_id` is provider-specific → suggest renaming to `provider_price_id` when the enum widens (filed in `packages/billing/REQUESTS.md`). (6) TS7-native did NOT auto-discover the root `@types/node` from this package's pnpm symlink (got TS2591 on `node:crypto`), unlike mesh which resolved it hoisted — billing added `@types/node` as its own devDep **and** `tsconfig types:["node"]` (small lockfile entry).
2026-07-19 · packages/billing (wt-billing follow-up, post-merge) — enum amendment landed + webhook insert wired + a HIGH money bug fixed. (1) **Enum amendment on main**: wt-db's `20260718193834_widen_billing_provider` widened `BillingProviderSchema` and the `subscriptions` / `billing_webhook_events` provider CHECKs to `['stripe','razorpay','cashfree','fixture']` (additive; pushed to the live DB). On that base, billing now writes the `billing_webhook_events` row it had deferred: a `WebhookEventStore` (pg) does `insert … on conflict (provider,event_id) do nothing` — a not-yet-'processed' duplicate is re-driven, a 'processed' one skipped — and `processPaymentEvent` composes claim→applyPlanGrant→mark. This is provider-level replay dedup + audit ON TOP of the ledger's `monthlyGrantKey` (two layers, both proven by real-DB tests). `ParsedWebhookEvent.provider` narrowed `string`→`BillingProvider`. (2) **HIGH double-charge fixed** (merge-checkpoint review, 3/3 verifiers): `LatestHold.settled:boolean` conflated settled-by-DEBIT (already charged) with settled-by-RELEASE (safe to advance) — a committed-but-lost-ack DEBIT (surfaced as PROVIDER_ERROR) made a retry advance the attempt and charge AGAIN. Now `LatestHold.settledBy: 'debit'|'release'|null` (from the settling entry's type); `nextAttempt` advances ONLY on 'release', so a lost-ack retry reuses the attempt and the DEBIT replays idempotently — **exactly-once charge per (action, objectRef); to charge again, pass a fresh objectRef**. The old test that asserted charged-twice (balance 98) was the bug enshrined; it now asserts 99. (3) **STILL OPEN — `provider_price_id` rename**: the enum amendment did NOT rename `plans.stripe_price_id` (still `stripe_price_id` in the migration + `PlanSchema`); the provider-agnostic rename to `provider_price_id` remains a `[contract]` TODO for wt-db/shared (billing reads no price_id yet, so non-blocking; tracked in `packages/billing/REQUESTS.md`). (4) **STILL OPEN — H19 TLS/CA hardening**: `pg.ts` keeps the `SUPABASE_DB_CA_CERT`→full-verify-else-encrypted-unverified-for-Supabase-host fallback (host-anchored). Ship/require the Supabase CA cert in prod so `rejectUnauthorized:true` — the direct endpoint's private CA can't be chain-verified without it.

2026-07-19 · packages/publishing (wt-pub) — OAuth handlers shipped; **wt-web mount contract**: import `createXOAuthHandlers` / `createGbpOAuthHandlers` + implement the `ConnectionStore` port (`upsertConnection(record)` → writes `connections` row + `record.encryptedSecret` VERBATIM into `connection_secrets`; it's an opaque vault-sealed string — never parse/log/return it). Mount thin routes: authorize route calls `beginAuthorize()` → redirect to `.url`, stash `{state, codeVerifier}` (X) / `{state}` (GBP) in an httpOnly cookie; callback route replays them into `handleCallback()` and branches on the shared `Result` (`VALIDATION_ERROR` → restart CTA, `PROVIDER_ERROR` → provider-side message). GBP extra: `handleCallback` may return `kind:'choose_location'` — show the picker, then `completeConnection({resumeToken, locationName,…})` within 10 min (resume token is vault-sealed; the browser never holds tokens). Env: `X_CLIENT_ID/X_CLIENT_SECRET`, `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID/GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET`, `TOKEN_VAULT_KEY`. Gotcha: OAuth callback flows are multi-call → use `routedTransport` (per-URL fixtures), not the blind single-response `fixtureTransport`. wt-pub

2026-07-19 · packages/db (wt-db) — `public.resolve_brand_memory` shipped (SECURITY DEFINER Brand Brain write RPC answering wt-web's blocker: role-allowlisted members, per-workspace advisory lock, supersede-then-insert versioning, content-based replay, optional CAS via `p_expected_version`). Decisions: (1) idempotency is content-based (jsonb equality against the active row) so it needs NO new column — but the CAS check runs BEFORE the replay branch, so an identical resubmit carrying a stale expected_version conflicts rather than replays; documented in the `@sahoda/shared` doc comment because that is wt-web's only reference, not the migration. (2) `system` → `resolved|manual` on identical content DOES mint a version — provenance is the change; the reverse direction replays, so a fallback can never relabel a genuine brain. (3) `max(version)` must span ALL statuses: `unique (workspace_id, version)` is unconditional and the CHECK admits `draft`, so an active-only max collides. (4) Guards must run BEFORE the advisory lock or any authenticated stranger makes the server take an arbitrary workspace's lock; the `v_ws_id` data dependency enforces that structurally (reordering yields a null lock key), and nothing after the membership select reads `p_workspace_id` again — cross-tenant writes are impossible by construction, not by check. (5) The SQL shape guard pins arrays + signal_lock only, NOT scalars or element types: zod stays the authority, and re-implementing BrandMemoryPayloadSchema in SQL would break single-source-of-truth. (6) Payload capped at 32 KB / 40 list entries because brand_memory feeds the mesh brand-context prefix prepended to EVERY model call — an oversized brain bills provider tokens forever against a fixed credit price. Gotchas: (a) Postgres guards are not NULL-total by default — `jsonb_typeof(null) <> 'object'` is NULL, not true, so an explicit JSON null (PostgREST forwards it verbatim; only an OMITTED arg takes the default) walks past a naive check into a raw 23502; use `is distinct from` / `coalesce`. (b) Mutation-tested all 28 live tests: 11 injected regressions, 11 caught — but three separate null-permeability mutations stayed GREEN because the guard is null-total in three independent places, so "test still passes" needed the whole validation block neutered to prove teeth. (c) Known redundancy shipped: `brand_memory_workspace_version_idx` is already covered by the frozen `unique (workspace_id, version)` btree — a later migration should drop it AND the frozen `(workspace_id)` index; not fixed in place because editing an applied migration is forbidden. (d) Adding a 5th live test file pushed `packages/db` past vitest's 5s default `testTimeout` and surfaced as 4 timeouts — two of them in the untouched `bootstrap.test.ts`. These suites hit the real Supabase over the network with files running in parallel, so the default was always wrong; added `packages/db/vitest.config.ts` (30s test+hook timeout). A live-DB timeout looks exactly like a logic failure — check the clock before the SQL. wt-db

2026-07-18 · apps/web (wt-web H2–6, second window) — workspace switcher + auth round-trip verification + deploy-preview prep. Findings: (1) Auth round-trip is BLOCKED on localhost by config, not code: the `.env` Clerk keys are PRODUCTION (`pk_live`, locked to `sahodalabs.com`) → FAPI 400 "HTTP Origin must be equal to or a subdomain of the requesting URL"; the in-app `<SignIn>` client fails to init on `localhost` and a completed sign-in can't redirect back. Env IS loaded (Next logs `Environments: .env.local`), keys ARE valid, middleware protection works — but local dev needs a Clerk DEVELOPMENT instance (`pk_test`, allows localhost). Same constraint hits deploy preview: a `*.vercel.app` preview is rejected too — the preview must serve from a `sahodalabs.com` subdomain (or use a dev instance / add the origin in Clerk) or auth breaks. There is NO guard distinguishing live/test keys (parseEnv only checks presence) — a `pk_live`-in-dev warning would catch this class early. (2) `auth.protect()` resolves its redirect from the MIDDLEWARE `signInUrl` (or `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_SIGN_IN_URL`), NOT `<ClerkProvider signInUrl>` (client-only) — without it protect() bounced to the unthemed hosted Account Portal, orphaning the in-app (auth) pages; fixed by passing `{ signInUrl:'/sign-in', signUpUrl:'/sign-up' }` to `clerkMiddleware`. (3) Switcher: reads memberships via the RLS-scoped client (empty until wt-db `bootstrap_workspace`), degrades to an honest "Create workspace" affordance whose `createWorkspace` action returns a typed `BOOTSTRAP_PENDING` (never a fake success); active workspace = an httpOnly cookie that is a UI pointer only (RLS is the real boundary, so an unknown/stale slug just falls back to the first membership). Topbar is now a dynamic server component (reads cookies+auth); supabase-js logs a benign "Failed to set initial Realtime auth token" during prerender (the route simply renders dynamically at runtime). Prod build green (exit 0, 9 routes).
2026-07-18 · apps/web (wt-web H2–6, first window) — scaffold + shell + Clerk + data seam. Patterns worth keeping: (1) Tokens-only is COMPILE-enforced, not lint-enforced: Tailwind v4 `--color-*: initial` wipe + `@theme inline` mapping of the shared vars makes any non-token color utility fail to compile; `@theme inline` (not plain `@theme`) is mandatory for var-referencing values or dark/Brand-Skin scoping breaks. Loophole to close later: arbitrary values (`text-[25px]`) bypass the gate. (2) `typedRoutes: true` makes dead nav links a typecheck error — cheap honest-UI enforcement. (3) Env validation = pure `parseEnv` (unit-tested, names-only error via a "never echoes values" test) + thin `server-only` boot module — but the boot module must be imported from a real server entrypoint or fail-fast is vacuous. (4) Clerk↔Supabase third-party = supabase-js `accessToken: () => auth().getToken()` (NO JWT template, NO @supabase/ssr); signed-out → anon → RLS denies; `supabase.auth.*` unreachable by construction. (5) Dark gotcha: `--t50/--t100` tint surfaces stay warm-light in dark while `--acc` flips to Orange300 — pairing them is ~1.7:1; accent-on-tint needs a `dark:bg-s2` surface swap. (6) Gotchas: hoisted TS7-native breaks `next-config-ts` (`ts.sys` undefined) → pin TS 5.9 in apps/web only; git worktrees make Next infer the wrong root → pin `turbopack.root`; pnpm 11 build-script approval lives in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` `allowBuilds`. BLOCKED dependency filed: web cannot bootstrap workspaces — needs wt-db `public.bootstrap_workspace` SECURITY DEFINER RPC (no INSERT policies on workspaces/workspace_members/users_profile; `app.apply_ledger_entry` is service-role-only in a non-PostgREST-exposed schema).
2026-07-19 · apps/web (wt-web, brand-resolve window) — onboarding + Brand Resolve shipped ("approve, don't author": Spark → Generate → Refine editable cards → live logo Theme), verified live end-to-end on a Clerk DEV instance. Rulings/patterns: (1) **Charge policy**: `resolveBrand` runs `brand_guidelines` (mesh) inside `withCredits({action:'brand_research'})`; the wrapped fn RETURNS only on a real resolve (→ DEBIT 50cr) and THROWS on a demo-fallback or error (→ RELEASE, no charge — users never pay for a non-resolve), carrying the flagged fallback payload out-of-band via a captured var so it's shown honestly (never as a genuine resolve). Proven in the ledger: GRANT 100 → HOLD 50 → DEBIT 50, balance 50, held 0, **0 brand_memory rows**. (2) **Persistence still BLOCKED**: `brand_memory` has read-only RLS + no write RPC; Finish returns a typed `SAVE_PENDING` (the `BOOTSTRAP_PENDING` pattern — brain held in session, no fake "saved v1"). Precise `public.resolve_brand_memory` spec filed in `apps/web/REQUESTS.md`. (3) **Live theming with ZERO other lanes**: Brand Skin overrides EXACTLY 7 upstream vars (`--p/--pfg/--pstrong/--acc/--t50/--t100/--t300`); extract logo colors client-side (canvas quantize → `oklch()` strings) and set those 7 as an inline `style` on the preview root — because `--color-primary: var(--p)` etc. are `@theme inline`, every `bg-primary`/`text-accent`/`bg-tint-*` inside re-themes live. **Readability Guard**: darken the extracted primary in steps until white OR `var(--ink)` clears 4.5:1, pick the better; near-black+white fallback for pathological inputs — the `--p/--pfg` pair is NEVER < 4.5:1 (tested). (4) **Turbopack gotcha**: `export type { X }` re-export from a `'use server'` module compiles under `tsc` + webpack `next build` but Turbopack (`next dev`) emits a runtime reference → `ReferenceError` at module load → 500 on every route importing the action. A 'use server' module may export only async functions; import the type from its source module. (5) **Dev auth**: each worktree has its OWN `.env` (`apps/web/.env.local → ../../.env`); localhost needs a Clerk DEVELOPMENT instance (`pk_test`) there — the `clerkKeyWarning` boot guard flags a `pk_live` key in dev. Clerk bot-sign-up Turnstile blocks headless sign-UP (managed challenge auto-passes for headed Chromium); dev test identity = `you+clerk_test@…` + OTP `424242`.
2026-07-19 · apps/web (wt-web, pre-PR review + persistence wiring) — the review caught a money bug that tests, typecheck and a live green run had all missed. (1) **BLOCKER: never let a client supply a ledger idempotency key.** `resolveBrand` took `objectRef` from a hidden form field. `withCredits` keys exactly-once on `(action, objectRef)` and `nextAttempt()` deliberately REUSES the attempt when the prior settled by DEBIT (its lost-ack protection) — so replaying a spent ref replayed the HOLD+DEBIT (no new charge) while the wrapped fn still ran the paid model call: unlimited `brand_guidelines` spend for one 50cr charge, exploitable from DevTools. Fix: `newResolveObjectRef(workspaceId)` — server-derived, fresh per call, and its signature takes no parameter a request body can reach; traceId likewise. Generalise: **any value that decides whether money moves must be derived server-side**, and the security property ("the action never READS this from input") is best pinned by a source-level guard test, since no amount of mocking can prove a negative. (2) **Don't pre-open a public route prefix for a route that doesn't exist yet.** `/api/webhooks(.*)` sat in the Clerk public matcher with no route mounted — a standing unauthenticated hole that would silently expose whatever landed there. Entries must be exact paths added WITH the route, which then verifies the provider signature in-route, accepts the LIVE provider only (the fixture HMAC secret is well-known → credit forgery), and never echoes a raw error. Failing closed (a missed entry breaks the webhook) is the safe direction. (3) **`resolve_brand_memory` is called with THREE arguments on purpose** — omitting the optimistic `p_expected_version` skips the CAS, so a rage-click on Finish replays the already-active payload as a success instead of raising VERSION_CONFLICT (precondition wins over idempotency, so passing a stale version would break the common case). The editors cap `banned_phrases`/`red_lines` at 40 (`lib/brand/limits.ts`) mirroring the RPC guard — not just to avoid INVALID_PAYLOAD, but because mesh prepends the active brain to every model call, so an oversized brain bills tokens forever. **A length cap is not a content check**: the live run proved it — exercising the cap left 36 BLANK red lines that saved happily (the RPC bounds array length, not emptiness; the clarity meter counts non-empty and so never noticed). Blank rows are one click to create and permanent once active, billing tokens on every future call and telling the Loop to refuse nothing. `pruneBlankListEntries` now strips blanks from the two OPEN lists before the RPC — never the three fixed-length arrays, which the contract pins at exactly 3 (filtering those would itself raise INVALID_PAYLOAD). (4) Repo hygiene: `LEARNINGS.md` on main carried COMMITTED conflict markers (`>>>>>>> wt-pub`, `>>>>>>> wt-db`) from earlier merges — resolve the tail by stripping markers and keeping every lane's line, and check `grep -cE '^(<<<<<<<|=======|>>>>>>>)'` before committing a merge.
2026-07-19 · packages/db (wt-db) — **shared-DB tests must use per-run-scoped idempotency keys; fixed keys + afterEach-only cleanup poisons the DB on interruption.** A run of `ledger.test.ts` killed between `beforeEach` and `afterEach` stranded a `ledger-test` workspace holding `grant:signup` and `inv:h1`; because `credit_ledger.idempotency_key` is **globally unique, not per-tenant** (`billing_ledger.sql:70`), those two literals were then permanently owned by a dead workspace and every later run failed — reproduced exactly: 8/9 red with `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT`. Fix: a `key(k) => `${k}:${ws}`` helper routed through `apply()`, mirroring `bootstrap_workspace`'s `'grant:signup:' || v_ws_id`, so every key carries that test's fresh workspace uuid and an interrupted run's leftovers can never be re-minted. Notes: (1) **The cross-workspace test is the one deliberate exception** — it reuses a key across tenants on purpose, so it captures the already-scoped literal via `key()` and passes it through verbatim rather than re-scoping. (2) **Scoping alone is the correctness fix; the cleanup pattern is the recovery fix** — added a `beforeAll` sweep of `created_by='user_ledger'` workspaces older than 1h (a heuristic, not a guarantee: a run parked at a breakpoint past 1h would be swept, a deliberate trade against never sweeping) plus an `afterAll` net over a `created[]` array for when `afterEach` itself throws. Both verified independently: the fix passes 9/9 against the identical poisoned DB that failed 8/9, and the sweep auto-removed a backdated poison row. (3) **`afterEach` must reset `ws=''` and `key()` must throw on empty** — otherwise a failed `beforeEach` silently rebuilds the fixed literal `grant:signup:undefined`, re-introducing the exact bug; relying on a distant `NOT NULL` to catch it is too narrow a margin. (4) Deleting the stray needed the **workspace**, not the row: `credit_ledger`'s append-only trigger blocks a direct DELETE at trigger depth 1 but `block_mutations` returns early at depth > 1, so the `on delete cascade` from `workspaces` is the only sanctioned path. (5) An audit of all 5 suites against every globally-unique column found the other four already run-scoped via `Date.now()` — `ledger.test.ts` was the sole offender. (6) Unrelated pre-existing flake seen once: `bootstrap.test.ts` → PGRST303 `JWT issued at future`, because `mintJwt` sets `iat` to exactly now with zero leeway; backdating `iat` a few seconds would kill the class. wt-db
wt-web
2026-07-19 · apps/web (wt-web, posts editor + wallet window) — the block shipped, but the durable lessons are about what the gates could NOT see. (1) **A stable `objectRef` is a free-generation hole, not idempotency.** `withCredits`'s `nextAttempt()` REUSES the attempt when the last hold settled by DEBIT, so binding `objectRef` to `posts.id` would make the second "Generate variants" replay the spent HOLD+DEBIT — charging nothing while still running the paid mesh call. billing's own contract comment states the rule ("to charge again, the caller passes a fresh objectRef"); every re-triggerable AI action therefore mints a fresh server-derived ref per invocation (`lib/posts/object-ref.ts`), exactly like `newResolveObjectRef`. The recon flagged this as a conflict between two reports and it was resolved by reading `withCredits.ts:136-139` directly — **when two analyses disagree about money, go read the source, don't average them.** (2) **`tsc` + a full unit suite + `next build` can all pass on a feature no user can reach.** An adversarial review found the body textarea's `onBlur={() => setSelection(null)}` unmounted the rewrite toolbar on mousedown (focusout is discrete, so React flushes it synchronously) — click never fired, and tabbing to the buttons blurred it too, so a paid AI action was dead by mouse AND keyboard. A roving tabindex separately moved `aria-selected` without moving DOM focus, so ArrowRight twice landed on the same tab. Both passed 461 tests. The root cause was structural: apps/web had **no component test harness at all**, so nothing interactive was testable. Added a two-project vitest config (`lib` in node, `ui` in jsdom) and pinned both defects — then **mutation-verified them**: reintroducing `onBlur` fails 3 tests, removing the `.focus()` fails 4. A reviewer's "reasoned but unobserved" finding is a hypothesis until a test fails on the mutation. (3) **jsdom gotcha**: React's `onSelect` comes from its SelectEventPlugin, which recomputes selection on focus/mouse/key events — dispatching a bare `select` Event does nothing. Use `setSelectionRange` + `fireEvent.keyUp`. Also `@testing-library/jest-dom/vitest`'s matcher augmentation only reaches `tsc` if `vitest.setup.ts` is in the tsconfig `include`. (4) **A raw-source workspace package can be typecheckable in its own lane and broken in yours.** `@sahoda/publishing` compiles clean under `types:["node"]` but its `fetchTransport` passes `Uint8Array` to DOM `fetch`, which TS 5.7+ rejects (`Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>` is not `BodyInit`) — and because the barrel pulls `transport.ts` into the graph, the ENTIRE package was unimportable from apps/web, the very consumer its own comments say will mount it. Fixed with a cast whose target is **derived from `fetchImpl`**, since naming `BodyInit` fixes the consumer and breaks the package. Cross-lane packages need at least one consumer-side typecheck in CI or this recurs. (5) **Honesty forced three real scope cuts, and one at the read layer**: `post_publish_logs` is member-read + `block_mutations` and `decryptToken` is unexported, so apps/web can neither publish nor RECORD a publish — the publish action is a pure simulation that persists nothing and never sets `status='published'`. The fixture checkout URL (`fixture.local`) does not resolve, so top-up renders a labelled sandbox result instead of redirecting. Twin score has a price but no backend, so the badge is omitted rather than faked. And `toBalance` collapsing an unreadable row to zero is a material lie — "we could not read your balance" is a different claim from "you have no credits", so `readBalance` now returns null and the UI says so. **A pure row-mapper cannot distinguish absent from unreadable; that distinction belongs at the I/O edge.** (6) `@sahoda/db`'s 8 live-DB tests fail on `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT` against the shared dev database (fixed idempotency keys + leftover rows, no per-run isolation) — pre-existing and unrelated to this branch, but it means `turbo test` is not currently a clean repo-wide gate.
HEAD
2026-07-19 · apps/web (wt-web, media attach) — server-verified upload closes the last gap in the posts block. (1) **Direct browser→storage upload would make `validateMedia` theatre.** It checks mime against `spec.mediaTypes`, bytes against `maxMediaMB` and pixels against `imageDims` — and all three are attacker-controlled in a signed-URL upload, because `File.type` is whatever the browser was told to say. So the bytes come THROUGH a server action, `sniffImage` derives format and dimensions from magic bytes, and the decision runs on facts. Cost: `serverActions.bodySizeLimit` had to rise from the 1 MB default; it is pinned at 12 MB, derived from the largest channel cap (instagram 8 MB) plus multipart overhead. If media ever gets large, the answer is a signed upload URL plus a server-side re-verify, not a bigger limit. (2) **Byte parsers are where "looks right" hides real bugs.** The adversarial pass found a JPEG frame read OUTSIDE its declared segment length — a file declaring an empty SOF returned a confident `9999×8888` scavenged from the next segment, which sails past gbp's 250×250 floor. Same class in PNG (IHDR length unchecked) and WebP (VP8 start code unverified). Worse, a VP8L height-mask mutation SURVIVED the original suite because every fixture left the alpha/version bits zero — an ordinary transparent WebP would have reported height + 16384 and the tests would have stayed green. Fixtures that only exercise the happy encoding are not coverage; 30 mutations, 29 killed, is. (3) **A mutation that "survives" is often a bad mutation, not a good test — check which.** Replacing `sniffed.image.mime` with `file.type` left all 14 action tests green, which looked like solid coverage of the insert but was actually my sed hitting the wrong call sites. Targeting the insert alone went red; targeting ALL sites revealed a genuine gap (nothing pinned that the ACCEPT/REJECT decision used the sniffed type), fixed with a gif-announced-as-png case that gbp must refuse. Always confirm the mutation landed where you think. (4) **Scripted deletion of test files is dangerous.** Removing a dead `attachSummary` export with a python line-skipper silently ate several `describe` blocks and orphaned a test body; the suite still "passed" because the orphan was unreachable. Reconstructed and mutation-verified. Prefer an editor over a script when cutting test files, and re-run with a mutation afterwards to prove what survived is real. (5) Storage: bucket `media` is private with `(storage.foldername(name))[1] = workspace_id` as the RLS gate, so `mediaObjectPath` refuses any id that is not a plain uuid — traversal is impossible to construct, not merely unlikely. Previews are 1-hour signed URLs minted per request under the caller's JWT; a row that cannot be signed still renders, labelled, because hiding it would understate the post's media against the channel caps.

2026-07-19 · packages/publishing (wt-pub, `[contract]` ConnectionUpsert) — **SUPERSEDES the `encryptedSecret` mount contract in the entries above**: the port now ships TWO sealed blobs, `accessTokenEnc: string` (→ `connection_secrets.access_token_enc`, jsonb NOT NULL) and `refreshTokenEnc: string | null` (→ `refresh_token_enc`, nullable), sealed by TWO separate `seal()` calls. (1) **A one-envelope design was cryptographically impossible, not merely inconvenient**: both tokens sat under a single AES-GCM auth tag and the vault key never leaves Node, so `upsert_connection` could not split it in SQL; the fallback (bundle into `access_token_enc`, leave `refresh_token_enc` null) works but makes a column name lie about its contents, so it was rejected. When a port can't be mapped onto the live schema, fix the PORT — a downstream lane cannot decrypt its way out. (2) **`seal()` is safe to call N times with one key**: `encrypt()` draws a fresh 12-byte random IV per call and holds no cipher state across calls, so two seals of the same key are independent envelopes (and two seals of the SAME plaintext still differ — a test asserting `accessTokenEnc !== refreshTokenEnc` therefore catches a copy-paste bug without being flaky). (3) **A guard you add only at the new call site is half a fix**: `sealSecret`/`sealOptionalSecret` in `oauth/common.ts` now wrap EVERY seal — including the pre-existing unguarded GBP resume-token seal — so a vault misconfig returns PROVIDER_ERROR on every path instead of raw-rejecting only on multi-location. (4) **`null` ≠ sealed-empty-string**: a grant without a refresh token is normal, so `normalizeOptionalToken` collapses both absent AND `''` to `null`; sealing `''` would store a valid-looking envelope over a non-credential. (5) Wire note for the mount: each blob is a `JSON.stringify`'d envelope but the RPC params are `jsonb` and reject anything whose `jsonb_typeof` isn't `'object'` — `JSON.parse` each AT THE RPC BOUNDARY ONLY (a structural cast, never a field read). 124/124 tests, tsc clean.

Exact-matching `'All changes saved'` fixed both. A regex over status copy will eventually match the failure state; match the success string exactly. 2026-07-20 · apps/web (wt-web, Vercel unblock) — the deploy "died at Linting and checking validity of types", and the types pass was innocent. (1) **The last printed phase banner is not the failing phase.** Next prints `Linting and checking validity of types ...` with no ✓ before moving on; the throw actually fired in "Collecting page data" — reproduced locally by building with `.env.local` removed, which produced the identical death on BOTH wt-web and main. The cause: `lib/env.ts` ran `parseEnv(process.env)` at module scope, and page-data collection imports every server module's graph (even `/_not-found`'s), so a Vercel project with no env vars configured fails the whole build before rendering a page. (2) **Fail-fast at first IMPORT and fail-fast at first ACCESS are different contracts, and only the second survives a build.** `env.ts` now parses lazily behind a Proxy (existing `env.X` call sites unchanged); boot warnings ride the first parse. Proven by experiment, not assertion: a production build with ONLY the three `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` vars and no server secrets exits 0 — so build-time requirements are exactly the inlined client vars, and `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` + mesh keys are runtime-only. (3) `server-only` cannot be imported by vitest in plain node; an alias to an inert stub (`vitest.server-only-stub.ts`) keeps the poison-pill in production while making the boot module testable — laziness is pinned by a test that imports the module with a bare env and expects silence until first access. (4) `outputFileTracingRoot` now pins the monorepo root for webpack builds, mirroring the existing `turbopack.root` pin, so Vercel's function tracing sees the pnpm workspace instead of inferring from whichever lockfile it finds first. (5) **A mirror-target Proxy without mutating traps is a process-poisoner, and review caught what the author's read-path testing never would.** `get/has/ownKeys` over a live object with a bare `{}` target works for every read shape (spread, keys, JSON) — but one stray `env.X = 'y'` silently defines a phantom own-property on the target and every LATER read of that key throws the engine's invariant TypeError until process restart, and `Object.freeze(env)` (exactly what this repo's immutability rule invites) trips the ownKeys invariant instantly. All five mutating traps now throw a clear read-only error at the misuse site, test-pinned. Corollary: the `import '@/lib/env'` boot-guard in layout.tsx became a silent no-op the moment the parse left module scope — a side-effect import documents nothing once the side effect is gone; removed rather than left lying.

2026-07-19 · packages/db (wt-db) — **PostgREST allows a FIXED 30s of `iat` clock skew; test JWTs must be backdated well past it, not just "a few seconds".** A single `bootstrap.test.ts` failure (PGRST303 `JWT issued at future`) while the rest of the same run passed looked like sub-second skew, and the obvious fix was `iat: now - 5`. Measuring the live edge refuted that: `iat = now+30s` returns 200, `now+31s` returns 401 — PostgREST already grants a hardcoded 30s allowance. So (1) `now - 5` would have widened the budget 30s→35s, a cosmetic change against an event that by definition required **>30s** of divergence, and (2) minting at exactly `now` spends the entire 30s allowance on whatever skew exists at that instant, which is why one request in a run can fail while its neighbours pass. Fixed with `IAT_BACKDATE_SEC = 300` in `tests/helpers/db.ts`. 300s is not arbitrary padding: it clears the 30s window by 10× AND is the more production-faithful shape — a real Clerk token is routinely minutes old by the time it is presented, so a freshly-minted `iat` is the _unrealistic_ case. `exp` stays anchored to `now` so token lifetime is unchanged. **Do not "simplify" the constant back toward 30s — anything inside that window re-opens the flake.** Method note: the whole correction came from probing the live edge across a range of offsets rather than reasoning from the error string; an error message that says "issued at future" invites you to assume the margin is tiny, and it is worth one script to find out what the server actually tolerates. Dropping `iat` entirely also works (verified 200) but loses token-shape fidelity with production. wt-db
2026-07-19 · packages/db (wt-db) — **RLS matrix for the sites family + themes; a denial test is worthless until you prove the token it denies is actually authenticated.** Added the four-class matrix (same-tenant positive control · cross-tenant · cross-user · signed-out) for `sites`, `site_pages`, `site_sections`, `leads`, `workspace_themes` — 38 new tests, `packages/db` 57 → 95. Three disciplines, each closing a way the whole matrix goes green while testing nothing: (1) **Token liveness controls.** Every "member B cannot …" case is vacuous if B's token failed to mint or verify, because it silently degrades to anon and sees zero rows anyway. So B first reads its OWN site (proves the token is live AND that the policy discriminates on membership using one identity across two tenants), and B's insert path is exercised in its own tenant (proves the cross-tenant rejections are about tenancy, not a malformed payload). The no-membership token is pinned against `guide_tours` (`select to authenticated using (active)`, 6 seeded rows): an authenticated stranger sees the tours while seeing no tenant row anywhere, and anon sees none — that contrast is the only thing separating "stranger" from "silently anon". (2) **A denied SELECT is an EMPTY SUCCESS, never an error** — RLS filters rows, it does not raise, so `error` is asserted null rather than ignored; an error would make the row count vacuously 0. And a denied INSERT must be shaped so RLS is the ONLY possible rejecter: `sites.slug` is globally unique, `site_pages` is `unique (site_id, path)`, `workspace_themes` carries both `unique (workspace_id, version)` and a one-active partial index — collide with any of those and the insert fails for the wrong reason while the test still passes (the theme case uses version 99 + 'proposed' to dodge both). (3) **Mutation-tested, not assumed**: granting userB an `editor` membership in tenant A turned 15/15 denials red with 0 positive/liveness/anon tests affected. The first mutation run was noisier and the noise was the lesson — the sites DELETE case targeted `siteA`, so when the policy "regressed" the delete cascaded through the composite FK and destroyed the page/section fixtures, surfacing as bogus "positive control" failures two describes later while the real page/section denials passed vacuously. **Point destructive denial tests at a throwaway row**, or one regression rewrites another table's fixtures and the failure message lies about where the bug is. Contracts pinned: `leads` has SELECT+UPDATE policies only (form-route inserts are service-role), so members cannot insert or delete a lead even in their own tenant — a member INSERT that ever starts succeeding means someone opened lead forgery. **[contract] follow-up**: `leads.site_id` is a plain `references sites (id)`, NOT the composite `(site_id, workspace_id)` the rest of the schema uses (`site_pages`/`site_sections` do) — so a lead can reference a cross-tenant site. Not client-reachable today (no member INSERT policy, and no server route inserts leads yet), but the Sites v0 form route must verify `site_id` belongs to the workspace, or a later migration should add the composite FK. wt-db
2026-07-19 · packages/db (wt-db) — `public.upsert_connection` shipped (SECURITY DEFINER OAuth-callback write path requested by wt-web; `connections` has no INSERT policy and `connection_secrets` has RLS with zero policies, so this is the one client-reachable way in). **The sealed-secret ruling (wt-pub review finding #1) is settled: the RPC takes TWO opaque sealed blobs.** The decisive argument is not preference — "the RPC splits the envelope" is _cryptographically impossible_: both tokens sit inside ONE AES-256-GCM ciphertext under one auth tag and the vault key lives only in the Node process, so no SQL can separate them; only `@sahoda/publishing` can. Of the two survivors, the two-blob signature is a strict superset — it is exactly right under wt-web's recommended (A) (wt-pub seals twice, each column holds what its name says) and still works under (B) (one bundle passed as `p_access_token_enc`, refresh null), so **wt-db never has to change whichever way wt-pub goes and is not blocking on an edit to a shipped interface**. Blobs are jsonb rather than text so a key-rotation job can select on `access_token_enc ->> 'key_version'` without decrypting (03_TSD §3 wants that runbook); the mount JSON.parses the sealed string once to hand it over, which is not "opening" the envelope since no field is read. Decisions worth not re-litigating: (1) **role allowlist is `owner`+`editor` only — deliberately narrower than `resolve_brand_memory`** (which allows `approver`): binding a real external account that can post as the business is an integration action, not a content action. Widening later is safe; narrowing later breaks people, so start narrow. (2) **Secrets are written VERBATIM including a null refresh — do NOT "improve" the upsert into `coalesce(excluded.refresh_token_enc, connection_secrets.refresh_token_enc)`**: access and refresh must come from the SAME grant, and pairing a fresh access token with a previous authorization's refresh token yields a connection that silently cannot refresh. (3) `created_by` is preserved on re-auth (first connector, not latest re-auther). (4) **No advisory lock, unlike `resolve_brand_memory`** — that one serializes a read-then-write on `max(version)`; here `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` is atomic against `connections_ws_platform_account`, so a lock would only add contention. (5) `INVALID_SECRET` uses `jsonb_typeof(x) is distinct from 'object'`, which also rejects an explicit JSON `null` — a bare NOT NULL column check would have happily stored jsonb `'null'` as a useless non-secret. Gotchas: (a) **applying a migration outside `supabase db push` leaves `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` unaware of it**, so a later push re-applies it — harmless here (`create or replace` + `comment on` are idempotent) but the history lies; record the version+name row by hand when you apply directly. (b) Mutation-tested against the live function rather than trusted: removing the role allowlist, removing the membership check, letting `created_by` be overwritten, and coalescing the refresh token each turned the suite red (1/2/1/1 failures respectively) and the baseline restored to 10/10 — for a security boundary, "the tests pass" is not evidence until you have seen them fail. **[contract] follow-up for wt-pub**: to reach the target mapping, `ConnectionUpsert.encryptedSecret: string` should become `accessTokenEnc` + `refreshTokenEnc: string | null` (seal twice). Nothing breaks until then. wt-db
