Two of them were broken the whole time. `migration_integrity` declared a table named `as`: `declaredObjects()` strips `--` comments but not STRING LITERALS, and the RLS auto-enable trigger compares against the live literal list `('CREATE TABLE', 'CREATE TABLE AS', 'SELECT INTO')`. Nothing named `as` can ever exist, so that release gate could not have passed against ANY database, production included — it looked healthy purely because it had never run. Its two regression tests now run WITHOUT a database, or the next such break waits again for a box that may not exist. **THE ANSWER WAS TO BRING A DIFFERENT DATABASE, NOT TO RELAX THE GUARD.** `pgbox.mjs` boots a real PostgreSQL, installs the Supabase preamble the migrations assume — the three roles, `auth.jwt()` over the same GUC so the policies under test are the SHIPPED policies driven by a real claim, and `storage.foldername` — and applies all 43. It installs its own binary OUTSIDE the workspace so `pnpm-lock.yaml` never moves; PGlite stays right for the `*.pglite.test.ts` suites and cannot serve this one, which connects over TCP with a connection string. `SAHODA_PGBOX_URL` is deliberately NOT `SUPABASE_DB_URL`: both that and `DATABASE_URL` are set in the repo environment and both name production, so a gate reading either is satisfied by a variable merely existing. **A RECORDING POOL CANNOT PROVE IDEMPOTENCY.** `route.test.ts` drives the real handler over a fake whose `billing_webhook_events` insert returns the same row every time with no already-processed flag and whose `apply_ledger_entry` returns `replayed:false` unconditionally — so a second delivery takes the first-delivery branch BY CONSTRUCTION. "Deliver it twice and prove the second changes nothing" is a claim about ROWS. The replay must reuse the SAME timestamp and signature (re-signing makes it a different delivery) and must land inside the ±5 minute window (outside it the handler answers 401 and "nothing changed" is true for the wrong reason). Both idempotency layers killed by mutation independently: disabling the `alreadyProcessed` short-circuit reddens only the replay test, because `monthlyGrantKey` still holds the credits — which is the two-layer design working, and the reason the second mutant had to attack the key itself. Balances are read back from `credit_balances` over a SECOND pool, because reading back through the thing that did the writing proves only that it remembers its own argument. **EVERY ROUNDING THE CUSTOMER'S WAY, ASSERTED AS AN INEQUALITY.** MEASURED at 5031bp: charge 75414.69 → 75414, set-off 25104.69 → 25105, credits 1760.85 → 1761. `toBe(Math.floor(x))` alone would also pass on a value that needed no rounding, so the test asserts the fraction is non-zero AND the direction. Chargeback clamping proved against the real constraint: granted 1500, spent 1300, disputed 1500 → reversed 200, shortfall 1300, originals byte-identical — unclamped it violates `balance_held_le_total`, aborts, and records NOTHING, which is why an imperfect record beats an invisible one. Dunning re-reads the balance after EVERY transition rather than once at the end, because a claw-back that happened and was undone is still a claw-back. **AND THE GATE ITSELF WRITES TO PRODUCTION.** The ledger moved 152 → 153 entries and 2805 → 2905 credits during this run and it was MINE: `grant:signup:` for `sahoda.e2e.…+clerk_test`, at the minute my own Playwright `@smoke` run signed up. Every other half of `pnpm gate` is fenced off by `forbidden-target.ts`; the e2e half is not, so each gate run creates a real workspace and a real 100-credit entry in production — three of the newest entries today are gate runs, not customers. The rows do NOT accrete, and the reason is worth knowing: `credit_ledger.workspace_id` is `on delete cascade`, and `app.block_mutations` deliberately permits a CASCADED delete (`pg_trigger_depth() > 1`) so a workspace can be offboarded — so when the seeded-user fixture cleans itself up the ledger entry goes with it. MEASURED: the count sat at 153 across two readings while the newest entry moved from 21:40 to 05:01 — one added, one removed. I had written the opposite ("append-only, so it can only ever be compensated") and it was wrong for exactly the case the trigger carves out. What DOES linger is three fixture workspaces from runs the OOM killer ended before their cleanup ran. Invariants 9/9 PASS before and after, and the delta is accounted for rather than explained away. **AND THE E2E HALF OF THE GATE CANNOT BE AIMED.** `E2E_PORT` appears nowhere in `turbo.json`, and Turborepo 2.x defaults to `envMode: "strict"` — a variable absent from a task's `env` list is STRIPPED before the task runs. MEASURED: `E2E_PORT=3213 turbo run test:smoke` logged `next dev --turbopack --port 3100`; after adding `E2E_PORT` to `test:smoke.env` the same command logged `--port 3213`. The override had simply never arrived, which is the same strict-mode mechanism that ran `ledger.test.ts` against production on 2026-07-27 — a blanked override stripped, then refilled from the repo-root file. It matters because several worktrees share this machine: with the override inert every one of them lands on 3100, and `reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI` is true locally, so the second suite to start ATTACHES to the first worktree's server and tests a different codebase while every failure reads as an ordinary assertion failure. **I first wrote that up as having HAPPENED, and it had not — the correction is the lesson.** The 14-failed run's own log says `next dev --turbopack --port 3100`: it started its OWN server, and the other worktree's suite began two minutes after mine ended. What actually reddened it was contention — that run overlapped `turbo run typecheck lint test` on a 15 GB box shared with two other sessions, and that turbo run failed `@sahoda/web#test` in the same window while both vitest projects passed standalone (2584 + 647 = 3231, matching the clean run) with no kernel OOM. The turbo task went 27/27 the moment it ran alone. So the port defect is real and worth fixing, and the dramatic story I attached to it was inferred from a truncated 27-line log tail whose missing port line I read as evidence of attaching when it was evidence of nothing. **A log that does not mention the port is not a log that says which port.** Before believing any smoke failure, grep for `next dev --turbopack --port` and confirm both the port AND that the line exists at all; before believing a smoke failure is YOURS, check what else was running.

2026-08-22 · integration (wt-integrate2, 13 lanes into one branch) — **The best defects were in files the two lanes did NOT share, and the worst one would have shipped with every gate green.** `wt-onboard2` swaps the mount point for `/onboarding`, orphaning 23 files in one commit — measured by walking imports from all 154 real entry points before and after, not by assuming a directory looked dead. The money guard for QA #6 lived in one of them, its own tests still passed, and NOTHING on the new side named the behaviour: a gate cannot see a file stop being imported. The hole was also bigger after the swap — the old flow dispatched through `useActionState`, whose queue SERIALISES two presses into two charges, while `start` is a bare async function where they run concurrently — and there were FOUR entry points, one of them a button labelled "Skip for now" that fires the same 50-credit call. **The browser test for it passed with the guard deleted, and instrumenting the component is what settled it:** Next 15 serialises server actions through one client queue, so intercepting-and-hanging the first POST stops the other two from ever being SENT — `resolveOnboarding` was entered three times while one request left the browser. `route.abort()` drains the queue; armed 1, broken 3. **A guard whose scope quietly stopped matching the app is the recurring shape.** wt-flow's `connections.status` scanner reads source TEXT for `.eq('status','…')`; wt-fix1 moved BOTH call sites to `.in('status',[…])`, so merged it covered NEITHER file the defect was in, and only its own not-vacuous assertion noticed. `no-impossible-remedy` reported 25 green routes while six sections merged that week were absent from its list. `roadmap-honesty`'s header said FIVE over a list of TWO, with a paragraph underneath insisting the number "is part of the test" — nothing read it. **`wt-knowledge`'s quarantine refactor is the one to remember:** consolidating two call sites into one shared fence is the right design and it silently dropped `human` from the turn-marker regex and the neutralisation of attacker-controlled header attributes. The header loss failed 3 tests; `Human:` failed ZERO, because the suite's only injection fixture uses `system:` — a fixture exercising one member of an alternation certifies the whole alternation. **And the union is where the DPDP export was broken:** eight tenant tables missing from every export while each branch's manifest was internally complete. A dropped quote landing on `.in(status, …)` typechecked, because `apps/web` loads `lib: ["DOM"]` for client components and the DOM lib declares a global `status: string` — a server file referencing a browser global, with tsc silent. Three migrations from three lanes share version `20260821000000` and git merged them without a conflict; `schema_migrations` is keyed by version so only one can ever be recorded, and all three are applied. Recording a short count is not the repair: `clerk_id_remap` looked unrecorded and IS unapplied — its two functions do not exist in production.

2026-08-23 · packages/research + packages/mesh + apps/web + packages/db (wt-sec) — **The mutation harness reported 11/11 killed on a run that executed zero assertions, and it took four separate repairs before any finding in this audit could be trusted.** Three of them manufactured verdicts. A per-mutant `cwd` was read once and closed over, so every command ran from the repo root, where `pnpm vitest run src/ip.test.ts` finds no test files and exits 1 — which this file calls KILLED. A suite whose `beforeAll` threw printed `Tests 6 skipped (6)`, exited non-zero, and was scored the same way; that is also exactly what a worker crash looks like when three sessions share a machine, and the SKIP COUNT IS THE TELL. And `String.prototype.replace` treats `$$` in a replacement STRING as an escape, so every PL/pgSQL mutant wrote `$;` where it meant `$$;`, unbalanced the dollar quote, and killed itself with a syntax error — MEASURED, the same mutant gave `4 failed | 3 passed` applied by hand and `Tests 7 skipped (7)` through the harness. Two shipped specs were affected, `rls-enforced.mjs` and `jobs-integration-executes.mjs`, which between them are this repository's proof that tenant isolation and the ledger function bite; both re-run honestly at 9/9 and 3/3. The tell was printed on every row of every report the whole time — "no test summary found in the output" — and it was styled as a footnote. It is now a refusal: a run must show at least one test that PASSED or FAILED before its exit code means anything, and the one mutant that legitimately breaks a fixture declares `expectsNoTests` where a reviewer sees it. **The general rule: an exit code is not a verdict. A harness that cannot tell "the code broke" from "nothing ran" will report the second as the first, and it will do it silently, in the direction that makes you feel safe.** The same lesson arrived twice more from the other side. A mutant that disabled a byte cap did not FAIL — it allocated until the machine gave out, because the fixture streamed forever; a mutant that hangs is indistinguishable from a mutant still thinking, so the fixture is now bounded at 12.8 MB against a 200 KB cap and fails in a second. And two survivors had to be chased to the end before either meant anything: one was a real gap (counting `could_not_check` could not tell "we refused to look" from "we looked and it did not answer" — the assertion is now `transport: UnsafeUrlError`, which can only exist before a socket does), the other an equivalence proven with 3,000,075 comparisons and removed with the proof attached. A comment I wrote myself claiming a signed bitmask breaks `224.0.0.0/4` was measured wrong in the same pass and corrected in place.
2026-08-23 · packages/db + apps/web (wt-dpdp) — **The two things a person can lawfully demand, and both halves of the export were blind in the same place.** DPDP §11 and §12: a copy, and erasure. The copy existed; erasure was a paragraph asking the customer to email, and that paragraph was HONEST — measured from `pg_policies`, **26 of the 47 workspace-owned tables have no member DELETE policy**, `brand_memory`, `inbox_threads`, `inbox_messages` and `leads` among them, so a client-driven button would have deleted half of it and reported success. `public.erase_workspace` is what makes the button truthful: one `SECURITY DEFINER` transaction, the table list read from `information_schema` on every call rather than typed out, and the deletes run in PASSES with per-statement savepoints instead of a fixed order — because ordering here is constrained by an `ON DELETE RESTRICT` **and** by `assets_refuse_delete_in_use`, a BEFORE DELETE TRIGGER that no ordering derived from `pg_constraint` could ever discover. **The `workspaces` row survives its own erasure**: all 47 foreign keys into it are `ON DELETE CASCADE` and three of those tables are the financial record, so deleting the row would erase what the law requires be kept. It is redacted and tombstoned instead. **`app.block_mutations` gains exactly one exception** — a DELETE, at depth 1, while `app.erasing_workspace` is set, never on `credit_ledger` or `invoices`, never an UPDATE — because four append-only tenant tables have no parent inside the erased set and can only be reached directly; and it hands a member nothing, because all nine guarded tenant tables have no member DELETE policy, which is asserted rather than argued. **THE FIRST VERIFICATION CONFIRMED ITSELF.** Erasure deletes `workspace_members`, so `app.member_workspace_ids()` immediately returns nothing and every later `select` the erasing member makes answers ZERO ROWS for every table in the database — the retained ledger read empty, the OTHER WORKSPACE read empty, and an erasure that deleted nothing at all would have passed every count. The verification now drops to superuser with `reset role` before it counts, and an assertion fails if it ever stops. **The export's blind spot is structural, not an oversight**: a sweep for `workspace_id` cannot see a table owned by a different key, and three held personal data — `workspaces` (the business name), `users_profile` (the customer's own EMAIL, which docs/31 said "belongs to Clerk" for four days), and `connection_secrets` (OAuth tokens, which are now NAMED as a permanent omission rather than absent). **The same blind spot bit the erasure**: it deleted only the caller's profile while three separate places claimed "anybody left with no workspace", and the fixture could not tell the two implementations apart until a second member of the SAME workspace was seeded. **Nine mutations killed on the guards, and the tenth found the hole they had all missed** — every one had broken a GUARD; none had broken a DELETE, so "one transaction, so a failure undoes everything" rested on the function being written inside `begin`. A refusing trigger now drives `ERASURE_INCOMPLETE` and all 48 tables are asserted intact afterwards. `export-drift.test.ts` had **never executed** since it was written on 08-19; run against production it says the manifest is TRUE of the live database, and its `set default_transaction_read_only` is replaced with `begin read only` because a session-level SET through the pooler is handed to the next client. And `docs/38`'s own table count said 47 while the branch had 48 — twenty minutes after the paragraph warning about stale numbers was written — so both the count and the list are now asserted against the catalogue on every build. **§7 was drafted from what the architecture implies and six of its claims were wrong against the code**, two of them gaps: **erasure does not reach Zernio** (no delete call exists anywhere, so the publishing profile and uploaded media survive), and **`billing_webhook_events` keeps the payer's name and phone** in a table with no `workspace_id` — outside the export sweep AND outside the erasure. The residency claim was withdrawn entirely: the only evidence that the database is in Mumbai is a comment in a test file.

2026-08-23 · wt-dpdp, FOR WHOEVER MERGES IT — **Three facts a merge needs and cannot see from the diff.** (1) `20260823000000_dpdp_erasure.sql` does `create or replace function app.block_mutations()`, so a lane that re-applies `20260718000001_helpers.sql` AFTER it silently restores the original and takes the erasure's exemption away. MEASURED by adding exactly that migration: `packages/db/tests/erasure.pglite.test.ts` goes red on FOUR tests and the message names the tables that became unerasable — **that file is the load-bearing test for this collision**, so a merge that keeps theirs over it loses the only thing that would notice. (2) `ledger_actor_redactions` is a NEW tenant table with RLS on and no policies, so it is declared in `EXPECTED_SERVICE_ONLY` in `rls_tenant_isolation.pglite.test.ts` and in `export-manifest.ts`; a merge that takes the other side of either file drops the declaration and that suite fails naming it, which is the correct behaviour and not a flake. (3) **The migration is deliberately NOT applied** — `packages/db/supabase/migrations` is wt-db's, and prod's behaviour without it is a real sentence ("Deleting a workspace is not switched on for this database yet") rather than a dead end, so there is no urgency to apply it as part of a merge. **And nothing in this lane has ever run against a real database**: the create → populate → erase → count cycle is PGlite, the storage sweep has only faced a stand-alone fake, and the one thing that did touch production was a read-only `begin read only` schema query. The eleven decisions in `docs/38 §6` are the owner's, not a merger's — the two that would change what a customer is told are that erasure does not reach Zernio and that `billing_webhook_events` keeps the payer's name and phone outside both rights.

2026-08-23 · apps/web /home + /analytics (wt-page-dash) — **The accent was not over budget where four documents said it was, and the fraction everyone had been quoting cannot see the defect it was quoted for.** docs/37 §2.3 puts /home at 0.487% saturated and /analytics at 0.498%, and neither is usable as a before: the Pillow script that produced them never landed in the repo, so a before and an after had to come from one new instrument or the difference would measure the methods. **The denominator is the whole argument** — a fullPage frame's height is the page's height, this lane's job was to make two pages SHORTER, and collapsing five cards into one statement would therefore have RAISED the fraction on a real improvement while padding the page "fixed" it. Measured from a fixed viewport instead, at 24 compositions (2 routes x 390/1024/1440 x light/dark x empty/populated): **/analytics populated reads 0.139% brand at 1440, a fifth of the quoted figure, and the number that is genuinely over is 390 — /home populated at 3.527%, six times its own 1440 figure, on the device these users actually hold and the one no previous pass measured.** The useful metric turned out not to be the fraction at all but **disjoint blobs of accent: 321 -> 145 across the matrix, /home 1440 dark 30 -> 6.** A page with thirty separate orange things has no focal point whatever its total is, and 0.3% in one button and 0.3% across nine links score identically. **Two claims I made out loud before measuring them were wrong and are corrected in place.** The greeting banner's wash is not the page's biggest accent spender — at 16% alpha it composites BELOW the s>0.30 floor and contributes approximately zero; 80% of /home's accent at 1440 is the Create post button, and the banner is a visual-dominance defect (1132x190 holding two words, 20% of a 390 viewport) which is a different fix at a different element. And **the mascot is not clipped by its container**: `public/mascot/0.png` is 2048x983, `bg-contain` in a 521x190 box renders all of it, and the plinth is cut off IN THE SOURCE ASSET — docs/27 §1 points at the CSS, and a lane "fixing the container" would have shipped a diff and re-photographed the same robot. **The 2026-08-20 /analytics fix was aimed one state too early.** Its gate is `not-connected && !hasPublished && posts.length === 0`, and the third clause was added because a two-part gate turned `analytics-history.spec.ts` red; measured one step along — four posts, two channels published, which is where a beta account sits after its first hour — the five-apology screen returns in full and it is six, across 1237px at 1440 without one number on it. **The gate was not widened**: the spec asserts the performance-over-time card is present and says "has started keeping a history" on exactly that workspace, so the DIAGNOSIS moved instead (one `analyticsReadiness` computed once, stated once at the top, children take `reasonStated` and fall back to slot marks) and the guard stayed green on its own terms. /home had the same defect unnamed and worse: **seven statements of one absence in six visual languages over 2025px at 390**, now one screen of 844px. **docs/37 §16 contradicts docs/37 §9 if you read it literally, and the guard is what found it** — "exactly one solid-brand-fill element per view" against `.is-real` being a solid brand fill, so three published posts break the rule by design; §2.3 states it in the words that resolve it ("one primary ACTION per view") and the guard counts `<a>`/`<button>`, not elements. It is scoped to `#main` for the same class of reason — below 700 the shell paints a permanent FAB on every route — and the assertion bounding what that scoping excludes immediately found the topbar restating the page's own absence. **A mutant survived and the guard was not at fault: the mutation missed its target.** Restoring the banner's duplicate `Create post` landed on a component the empty-workspace walk never renders, which is "nothing broke / nothing ran" with extra steps; the populated walk exists because of it and kills that mutant now. **And `charts.test.tsx` asserted a defect as a feature** — "a single action still renders a sane bar" required the always-100% track, and `width > 0` passes as happily on 20 credits as on 20,000, because with one category `credits / peak` is `credits / credits`. That bar was the largest solid-orange area in the product outside a button and it encoded a number already printed at its right-hand end. **Three more defects only a frame found**: `Sahoda: Sahoda:` on every empty /analytics at every width in both themes (`EmptyState` prefixes the tip, the caller passed one already carrying the prefix); a "Details" link pointing at /analytics while rendering ON /analytics, the quietest dead end there is; and `overflow-hidden` shaving the left bearing off the "G" of "Good evening" once padding went to 0 below narrow, visible only in a 3x crop. Ratchet: 838 -> 755 hand-written font sizes, shown refusing a regression. `follower-chart.tsx` is deliberately NOT converted — none of its populated paths reaches a captured frame, and changing what cannot be looked at is a change nobody can report on. Production rows left by three capture passes and two mutation rounds: **zero**, counted afterwards through the service-role client rather than assumed.
2026-08-23 · apps/web (wt-page-flow) — **The ninth click did not exist, and the reason four screens shouted was that nobody had asked which thing was blocking.** `docs/34` measured journey 1 at eight clicks from nothing to a saved draft and then found no ninth: scheduling was a bare `dd/mm/yyyy, --:--`, the only unstyled control on the screen, so the journey's stated goal was unreachable by looking for a reader who has never used a marketing tool. It is now three NAMED times, each PRINTING the exact instant it means — a shortcut to a time, never advice about one, because there is no best-time backend and `docs/37 §17` forbids rendering a number the product cannot prove. Every candidate is filtered against the channels' own lead; that filter **cannot fire today** (all four channels declare 5 minutes, the soonest choice is an hour out) so it was split out and proved at floors no channel reaches, rather than shipped as untested defensive code. **+0.1 kB, no dependency.** And the picker now states the DELIVERY RANGE — "between 6:00 and 6:07 pm, Sahoda checks every 5 minutes" — derived from `delivery-window.ts` and asserted to be no tighter than the window `autoPublishTruth` refuses to call late, so the screen cannot promise a range and then decline to flag a post that missed it. **The hierarchy fixes all came from one question**, `docs/37 §16`'s first rung: is the user BLOCKED? `ConnectFirstNote` was a full-width wash with a ring and a button on two routes whose own copy says _"you can write and plan without one"_ — so it led screens it did not block, and the previous pass had demoted its button while writing, accurately, that "the banner is still the loudest thing in its own right". That is the finding, not the mitigation. `/planner`'s paid panel sat ABOVE the plan at desktop widths because the founder's "plan comes first" ruling had been applied `max-narrow` only — and MEASURED at 1440 the cost is worse there, not better: on `?view=month`, a view reached by clicking _Calendar_, the calendar began at y≈580 of a 900px viewport. **All 108 frames: 2.218% → 1.625% saturated; /planner 3.9% → 2.1%, and the absolute pixel count fell with the fraction, which is the test that distinguishes a real reduction from a shorter page.** The accent meter had to be BUILT — `docs/37 §2.3` states the method and the script that produced its figures is not in the repository — so it is calibrated against images whose answer is arithmetic and was shown red by flipping `>` to `>=`; it counts platform marks, and that limit is written down rather than discovered later. **The worst thing found was found by contact sheet, not by reading frames one at a time**: onboarding's disabled `Continue` measured **1.65:1 light / 1.75:1 dark** on real composited pixels — the same class of defect `docs/34` fix #2 called CRITICAL and fixed in `button.tsx` at 1.37:1 — and it survived because **these screens do not use the Button primitive**, so no amount of care in that component could ever have reached them. Pale-on-pale is unmissable beside three siblings that are not disabled and invisible one frame at a time. **And a gate lesson that cost two runs.** `turbo run build` is the gate's LAST stage and it rewrites `.next` **underneath the long-running `next start` that stage 3 reuses** — so a second consecutive `pnpm gate` against the same server failed stage 3 on SIX unrelated specs at once (`analytics-history`, `approvals` ×2, `assets`, `audience-layers` ×2), every one of which had passed forty minutes earlier on code differing only by a unit test and two documents. **Six unrelated failures together is an environment, not a diff.** Clear `.next`, rebuild, restart the server, and it is clean: 102 passed, 0 flaky. Watch the stage-1 duration too — a **0.6s** turbo leg is a cache replay verifying nothing, and it must be quoted as one rather than as a result. **Three harness lessons.** A vitest file under `e2e/` is collected by Playwright's default `testMatch` and a collection error takes the WHOLE suite: `--list` reported **0 tests in 0 files** and the gate's smoke leg reported it only through a stack trace. Five of six close-up "chosen" frames came back BYTE-IDENTICAL to their unchosen twin because the first click set a schedule that persisted — caught by hashing, invisible to a size check. And a `crop-geometry` timeout at 5131ms against a 5000ms limit was **contention, not the diff**: no kernel OOM, load average 16 from peer sessions, 28/28 in 2.14s standalone, and the file is not in this lane's diff at all. **A test that reaches a control by its old route is not a loosened guard**: four assertions in `schedule-field.test.tsx` reached the native input directly _because it was the only control the field had, which was the defect_ — every guarantee they asserted is unchanged and the named-time path ADDED eight guards rather than replacing any.

2026-08-24 · apps/web (wt-boot) — **A test that passed while exercising nothing, and a fixture that cost three files.** The blocked-video test installed `page.route` AFTER walking to the result step, but the element preloads there — so the 2.7 MB was already cached, the "blocked" run played the film normally, and **11,061 ms sailed under a `< 15s` assertion**. Moving the route BEFORE the walk is also what actually happens to a customer whose connection cannot fetch it, and it exposed a real gap: a file that had already errored during preload still made `start()` show the overlay and hold it for the full 2.5 s deadline — the watchdog rescued them, so never a dead end, just two and a half seconds of a brand-coloured rectangle for somebody whose connection is already having a bad day. `start()` now checks `video.error` first. **11,061 ms → 3,529 ms.** Separately: **Playwright runs a fixture only if the test DESTRUCTURES it.** Six tests in `onboarding-routing.spec.ts` wrote `async ({ page })`, ran signed OUT, and failed against /sign-in naming missing elements — which reads exactly like a product regression. 2026-08-27 · apps/web + packages/publishing (wt-divas, advisor, round 10) — **The founder asked why Instagram and LinkedIn work perfectly, and the answer explains every other bug in the lane: their name is the same string in all FOUR of Zernio's platform vocabularies.** MEASURED from `zernio.com/openapi.json` and the live API: connect, `/v1/accounts`, edit, unpublish and validate all say `twitter`/`googlebusiness`, while `POST /v1/posts` says `x`/`google`. So every name-shaped defect was **invisible on exactly the two channels anybody tests by hand** — `connectUrl` given `x` answered 400 on every press, `reconcileAccounts` matched `x` against a stored `twitter` so a completed connect wrote no row, and `health.ts` read X's two-hour rotating token as a sixty-day deadline. Three separate reports, one cause, and the two channels that would have revealed it were the two immune to it. `vocabularies.test.ts` now pins all four side by side and asserts they **DISAGREE**, because the tempting tidy-up unifies them and a tidy-up that rewrites two equality checks would still pass a pair of equality assertions. **RETRACTED mid-investigation, and the retraction is the lesson:** I found `ZERNIO_PLATFORM_NAME` mapping `x: 'x'` and `gbp: 'google'` and was about to correct it as the same bug a fourth time — `recovery.ts` records both as `[LIVE]`-measured with real posts having gone out through those exact strings, and the publish endpoint genuinely is the odd one out. This repository already carries "an absence in our client is not an absence in their API"; the mirror image is now proven too, that a value which looks wrong beside three others may be the only one that works, and the check costs one grep of the file that owns it. **Separately, the focus fallback shipped two rounds earlier could never have fired.** `popupCloser` posts on the BroadcastChannel and THEN calls `window.close()`, so the message lands while the tab is still behind the popup: `finish()` runs, `pending` goes false, the pending-gated effect is torn down and the focus listener is REMOVED — all before the popup disappears and before focus returns. The safety net was taken down a moment before the fall. **The test could not see it because it dispatched `focus` with no message first**, which is not the order the browser runs in, so it passed against a listener that cannot work — the same class as the vocabulary guard that did not guard, one lane earlier. The fix records an OWED repaint when `document.hasFocus()` is false (`hasFocus`, not `visibilityState`: a tab behind a popup is still "visible", it simply lacks focus) and spends it from an effect that is deliberately NOT gated on `pending`. The old "stops listening once the wait is over" test was RETARGETED rather than deleted — the listener outliving the wait is now the point, and the guarantee it protected, no refresh on every later tab switch, is asserted directly. **No migration was written or applied and nothing was published.**
